&\leq C(T)\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}^{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{2\iota}\mathrm{D}_{r}^{2}u_{t}|_{2}^{2}+1\big)+\frac{a_{1}}{8}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{\iota}\big(u_{ttr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{tt}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2},\end{aligned}

(7.84)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} G_{23}&\leq\big(|u_{r}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}+2|u_{t}|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tr}|_{2}+|u|_{\infty}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{ttr}|_{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi_{ttr}|_{2}\big)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}\\
&\leq C(T)\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}^{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{2\iota}\mathrm{D}_{r}^{2}u_{t}|_{2}^{2}+1\big)+\frac{a_{1}}{8}\Big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{\iota}\big(u_{ttr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{tt}\big)\Big|_{2}^{2}.\end{aligned}

Thus, collecting (
7.80
)–(
7.84
), along with Lemma
7.2
, gives

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}^{2}+a_{1}\delta a|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{\iota}\mathrm{D}_{r}u_{tt}|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}|_{2}^{2}+|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{2\iota}\mathrm{D}_{r}^{2}u_{t}|_{2}^{2}+|\mathrm{D}_{r}^{2}u|_{\infty}^{2}+1\big).

Multiplying the above by
t
and integrating the resulting inequality over
[\tau,t]
for
\tau\in(0,t)
, along with Lemmas
7.2
,
7.11
, and
7.13
, imply

(7.85)

t|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}(t)|_{2}^{2}+\int_{\tau}^{t}s|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{\iota}\mathrm{D}_{r}u_{tt}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T)\big(\tau|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}(\tau)|_{2}^{2}+1\big).

Next, thanks to Lemma
7.10
and
r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(I))
, it follows from Lemma
A.6
that there exists a sequence
\{\tau_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
such that
\tau_{k}\to 0
and
\tau_{k}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}(\tau_{k})|_{2}^{2}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
. Choosing
\tau=\tau_{k}\to 0
in (
7.85
) yields

(7.86)

t|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}(t)|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}s|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{\iota}\mathrm{D}_{r}u_{tt}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

3.
In the final step, we give a rigorous proof of the energy equality (
7.80
). Suppose that
\boldsymbol{\varphi}(\boldsymbol{x})=\varphi(r)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{r}
is any given spherically symmetric vector function satisfying
r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\varphi,\mathrm{D}_{r}\varphi)\in L^{2}(I)
. By Lemma
B.1
, this is equivalent to
\boldsymbol{\varphi}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
Then applying
r^{m}\varphi\partial_{t}
to both sides of
\eqref{e2.2}_{2}
and integrating over
I
yield from (
7.1
) and integration by parts that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}u_{tt}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=-2a_{1}\delta a\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\phi^{2\iota}\big(u_{tr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{t}\big)\big(\varphi_{r}+\frac{m}{r}\varphi\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+2a_{1}\delta a\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}(\phi^{2\iota})_{t}\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)_{r}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+2a_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\Big(\psi_{t}\big(\delta u_{r}+m(\delta-1)\frac{u}{r}\big)+\psi u_{tr}\Big)\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\big((uu_{r})_{t}+\phi_{tr}\big)\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r.

Here, the process of integration by parts can be justified by following the similar discussions in (
7.38
)–(
7.40
).

Next, differentiating the above with respect to
t
leads to

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}u_{tt}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=-2a_{1}\delta a\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\phi^{2\iota}\big(u_{ttr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{tt}\big)\big(\varphi_{r}+\frac{m}{r}\varphi\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle-2a_{1}\delta a\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}(\phi^{2\iota})_{t}\big(u_{tr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{t}\big)\big(\varphi_{r}+\frac{m}{r}\varphi\big)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+2a_{1}\delta a\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\Big((\phi^{2\iota})_{tt}\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)_{r}+(\phi^{2\iota})_{t}\big(u_{tr}+\frac{m}{r}u_{t}\big)_{r}\Big)\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\Big(2a_{1}\Big(\psi_{t}\big(\delta u_{r}+m(\delta-1)\frac{u}{r}\big)+\psi u_{tr}\Big)_{t}-(uu_{r})_{tt}-\phi_{ttr}\big)\Big)\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r.

Based on the
a priori
assumption
r^{\frac{m}{2}}(u_{ttr},\frac{u_{tt}}{r},\phi_{ttr},\psi_{tt})\in L^{2}([\tau,T];L^{2}(I))
for
\tau\in(0,T)
,
and the similar calculations of
G_{20}
–
G_{23}
in Step 2, we obtain

\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\cdot\boldsymbol{\varphi}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}u_{tt}\varphi\,\mathrm{d}r\leq F(t)|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\varphi,\mathrm{D}_{r}\varphi)|_{2}

for some positive function
F(t)\in L^{2}(\tau,T)
, where
\omega_{n}
denotes the surface area of the
n
-sphere. Thus, it follows from Lemma 1.1 on
[
56
, page 250]
and Lemma
B.1
that
\boldsymbol{u}_{ttt}\in L^{2}([\tau,T];H^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
. Consequently, the energy equality follows from the spherical coordinates transformation and the following identity, due to Lemma
A.8
:

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}|u_{tt}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r=\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\frac{2}{\omega_{n}}\langle\boldsymbol{u}_{ttt},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\rangle_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

The proof of Lemma
7.14
is completed.
∎

Lemma 7.15
.

There exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that, for any
q\in[2\iota,0]
,

\sqrt{t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{q}(\mathrm{D}_{r}^{2}u_{t},\mathrm{D}_{r}^{4}u)(t)|_{2}\leq C(T)\qquad\text{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

Proof.

It follows from (
7.63
) and Lemmas
4.9
and
7.14
that

\sqrt{t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{q}\mathrm{D}_{r}^{2}u_{t}(t)|_{2}\leq C(T)\sqrt{t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{2\iota}\mathrm{D}_{r}^{2}u_{t}(t)|_{2}\leq C(T)\big(\sqrt{t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}u_{tt}(t)|_{2}+1\big)\leq C(T).

Then (
7.75
), together with the above, yields

\sqrt{t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{q}\mathrm{D}_{r}^{4}u(t)|_{2}\leq C(T)\sqrt{t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{2\iota}\mathrm{D}_{r}^{4}u(t)|_{2}\leq C(T)\big(\sqrt{t}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\phi^{q}\mathrm{D}_{r}^{2}u_{t}(t)|_{2}+1\big)\leq C(T).

∎

8.
Global Well-Posedness of Regular Solutions with Far-Field Vacuum

Based on the local well-posedness stated in Theorem
3.2
and the corresponding global uniform estimates obtained in §
4
–§
7
, we are now ready to give the proof for Theorem
2.1
. We divide the proof into three steps.

1. Global well-posedness of regular solutions.

First, according to Theorem
3.2
, there exists a
regular solution
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
of the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with (
1.6
)–(
1.7
) in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
for some
T_{*}>0
, which takes form (
2.8
).

Second, let
\overline{T}_{*}>0
be the life span of
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
,
and let
T
be any fixed time satisfying
0<T<\overline{T}_{*}
.
Then collecting the uniform
a priori
bounds obtained in Lemmas
4.3
,
4.9
, and
7.3
–
7.15
, together with (
7.1
) and Lemma
B.1
, yields that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,

(8.1)

\displaystyle\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1}(t)\|_{H^{2}}+\|\nabla\rho^{\delta-1}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}\cap D^{1,n}\cap D^{2}}+\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}(t)\|_{L^{2n}}\leq C(T),

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{H^{3}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{H^{1}}+\int_{0}^{t}\big\|(\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T),

\displaystyle\big\|\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}(\nabla\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t})(t)\big\|_{L^{2}}+\big\|\rho^{\delta-1}(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u})(t)\big\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\big\|\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T),

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\big\|(\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})(t)\big\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}s\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

Clearly,
\overline{T}_{*}\geq T_{*}
. Next, we show that
\overline{T}_{*}=\infty
. Otherwise, if
\overline{T}_{*}<\infty
,
according to the uniform
a priori
estimates (
8.1
) and the weak convergence arguments, for any sequence
\{t_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
satisfying
0<t_{k}<\overline{T}_{*}
and
t_{k}\to\overline{T}_{*}
as
k\to\infty
, there exist a subsequence (still denoted by)
\{t_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
and limits
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u},\bar{\boldsymbol{f}},\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
satisfying

\displaystyle\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\ \ \text{for any $p\in(1,\infty]$},\qquad\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{f}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),\qquad\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{1,n}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

and, as
k\to\infty
and for
p\in(1,\infty)
,

(8.2)

\displaystyle\rho(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle\text{weakly\ \, in }L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle\text{weakly\ \, in }H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

\displaystyle(\rho,\nabla\rho^{\delta-1})(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to(\rho,\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle\text{weakly* in }L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

\displaystyle\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\bar{\boldsymbol{f}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle\text{weakly \, in }H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\rho^{\delta-1}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle\text{weakly \, in }L^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

\displaystyle\nabla^{3}\rho^{\delta-1}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\nabla^{2}\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle\text{weakly \, in }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).

Then we claim

(8.3)

(\bar{\boldsymbol{f}},\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=(\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1},\nabla\rho^{\delta-1})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\qquad\text{for {\it a.e.} $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$}.

For simplicity, we prove that
\bar{\boldsymbol{f}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
for
a.e.

\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, since the rest of (
8.3
) can be derived analogously.

First, due to
\eqref{global-unifrom33}_{1}
, there exists a subsequence such that

(8.4)

\rho^{\gamma-1}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\bar{\phi}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\qquad\text{weakly* in $L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})$}

for some limit
\phi(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
On the other hand, it follows from
\eqref{global-unifrom33}_{1}
and Lemma
6.5
that,
for any fixed
R>0
,

(8.5)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\rho^{\gamma-1}(t)\|_{H^{2}(B_{R})}\leq C(R,T),\quad\quad\inf_{(t,\boldsymbol{x})\in[0,T]\times B_{R}}\rho(t,\boldsymbol{x})\geq C^{-1}(R,T)

for some constant
C(R,T)>0
depending only on
(C_{0},R,T)
,
where
B_{R}=\{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\,|\boldsymbol{x}|<R\}
.

Since
H^{2}(B_{R})
is compactly embedded in
C(\overline{B_{R}})
, by extracting a subsequence,
there exists a limit
0<\bar{\bar{\phi}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in C(\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that,
for each
R>0
,

(8.6)

\rho^{\gamma-1}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\bar{\bar{\phi}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\qquad\text{uniformly on $B_{R}$}\ \ \text{as $k\to\infty$}.

Clearly,
0<\bar{\phi}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\bar{\bar{\phi}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap C(\mathbb{R}^{n})
due to the uniqueness of limits in (
8.4
) and (
8.6
), which, together with
\eqref{global-unifrom33}_{1}
and (
8.5
)–(
8.6
), yields

(8.7)

\rho(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\bar{\phi}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\qquad\text{for any $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$}\,\,\,\text{as $k\to\infty$}.

Then it follows from the uniqueness of the limits in
\eqref{f2}_{1}
and (
8.7
) that
\bar{\phi}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
,
i.e.
,
\bar{\phi}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\rho^{\gamma-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
.

Next, it follows from
\eqref{f2}_{4}
, (
8.4
),
\bar{\phi}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\rho^{\gamma-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
, and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem that, for any
\zeta(\boldsymbol{x})\in C^{\infty}_{\rm c}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
i=1,\cdots\!,n
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\gamma-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\zeta_{x_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\gamma-1}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\zeta_{x_{i}}(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=-\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{x_{i}}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\zeta(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bar{f}_{i}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\zeta(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}.

This implies that
\rho^{\gamma-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
admits the weak derivatives
(\rho^{\gamma-1})_{x_{i}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\bar{f}_{i}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for
i=1,\cdots\!,n
, so that
\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=\bar{\boldsymbol{f}}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
for
a.e.

\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

We now continue to prove that
\overline{T}_{*}=\infty
. We aim to show that functions

(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
satisfy all the initial assumptions
given in Theorem
3.2
, which consist of showing that

(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
are spherically symmetric
and satisfy (
2.5
)–(
2.6
).

1.1.
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
are spherically symmetric.

It suffices to show that
\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
is spherically symmetric,
since the proof for
\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
can be derived analogously.
To achieve this, it suffices to show that

\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})
for any
\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
.
Indeed, since
\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})
is spherically symmetric for each
t_{k}
and, by (
8.2
),
\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})
converges to
\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
weakly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
as
k\to\infty
, it follows from the coordinate transformation that,
for any vector function
\boldsymbol{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\cdot\boldsymbol{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\cdot\boldsymbol{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u})(t_{k},\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})\cdot\boldsymbol{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\cdot(\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{\zeta})(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\cdot(\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{\zeta})(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})\cdot\boldsymbol{\zeta}(\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x},

which implies that

\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})
for any
\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
.

1.2.
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
satisfies (
2.5
)–(
2.6
).
It can be directly checked that (
2.5
) holds. Next,
\rho|_{t=\overline{T}_{*}}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
follows from (
8.1
),
\bar{\phi}^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}|_{t=\overline{T}_{*}}=\rho|_{t=\overline{T}_{*}}
in (
8.7
), and Lemma
A.5
:

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\liminf_{k\to\infty}\rho(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq\liminf_{k\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C(T).

To show (
2.6
), from (
8.2
)–(
8.3
), it suffices to prove

(8.8)

(\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}\nabla\boldsymbol{u},\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).

For brevity, we only sketch the proof of
\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. By
\eqref{global-unifrom33}_{3}
, denoting by
\{t_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
the same sequence as in (
8.2
), we see that
\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\boldsymbol{F}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
weakly in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. On the other hand, based on

(8.9)

\displaystyle(\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u})(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})-(\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=\big(\rho^{\delta-1}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})-\rho^{\delta-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\big)\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})+\rho^{\delta-1}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\big(\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})-\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})\big),

using
\eqref{f2}_{2}
and
\rho(t_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\to\rho(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
uniformly on
B_{R}
as
k\to\infty
due to (
8.6
), we can show that the right-hand side of (
8.9
) converges weakly to
\boldsymbol{0}
in
L^{2}(B_{R})
for each
R>0
. Thus, the uniqueness of the limits implies that
\rho^{\delta-1}\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}|_{t=\overline{T}_{*}}=\boldsymbol{F}|_{t=\overline{T}_{*}}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

1.3.

To sum up, we have shown that
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(\overline{T}_{*},\boldsymbol{x})
satisfies all the initial assumptions on the initial data of Theorem
3.2
.
Thus, by Theorem
3.2
,
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
can be uniquely extended to the regular solution of the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with (
1.6
)–(
1.7
) in
[0,\overline{T}_{*}+T_{**}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
for some
T_{**}>0
. This contradicts to the definition of
\overline{T}_{*}<\infty
. Therefore
\overline{T}_{*}=\infty
.

2. Proof of Theorem
2.1
(i).
First, it follows from (
8.1
) and the relation:

(\delta-1)\nabla\rho=\rho^{2-\delta}\nabla\rho^{\delta-1}
that, for any finite
T>0
,

\|\nabla^{2}\rho\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C_{0}\|\rho\|_{L^{6-4\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{3-2\delta}\|\nabla\rho^{\delta-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{2}+C_{0}\|\rho\|_{L^{n^{*}(2-\delta)}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{2-\delta}\!\|\nabla^{2}\rho^{\delta-1}\|_{L^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C(T),

which, along with the fact that
\rho\in C([0,T];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
, gives that, for any
t_{0}\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to t_{0}}\|\rho(t)-\rho(t_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\lim_{t\to t_{0}}\|\rho(t)-\rho(t_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{4-n}{n+4}}\|\nabla^{2}\rho(t)-\nabla^{2}\rho(t_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{2n}{n+4}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\lim_{t\to t_{0}}\|\rho(t)-\rho(t_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{4-n}{n+4}}=0.

This implies that
\rho\in C([0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}}))
.

Next, since
\nabla\rho^{\delta-1}
is a spherically symmetric vector function, it follows directly from Lemma
C.2
in Appendix
C
and
\nabla\rho^{\delta-1}\in C([0,T];D^{1,n}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
that
\nabla\rho^{\delta-1}\in C([0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}}))
, which thus yields
\nabla\rho\in C([0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}}))
due to
\rho\in C([0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}}))
and
(\delta-1)\nabla\rho=\rho^{2-\delta}\nabla\rho^{\delta-1}
.

Moreover,
\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];C^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}}))
follows directly from
\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
and Lemma
A.1
, and
\rho_{t}\in C([0,T];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}}))
owing to
\rho_{t}=-\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\rho-\rho\diver\boldsymbol{u}
and
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T];C^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}}))
.

Finally, thanks to (
8.1
), we have

(t\boldsymbol{u}_{t},t\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\qquad((t\boldsymbol{u}_{t})_{t},(t\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})_{t})\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

which, along with Lemma
A.8
, yields
(t\boldsymbol{u}_{t},t\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T];W^{1,4}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
. Therefore, it follows from the above and Lemma
A.1
that
(\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})\in C((0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

3. Proof of Theorem
2.1
(ii)–(iii).
We only give the proof for the 3-D case, and the 2-D case follows analogously. First, since
(\sqrt{\rho},\sqrt{\rho}\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
, then
\rho\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
and hence
r^{2}\rho u\in C([0,T];L^{1}(I))
.
Next, via the spherical coordinate transformations:
r\in I
,
\theta_{1}\in[0,2\pi]
,
\theta_{2}\in[0,\pi]
, and

\boldsymbol{x}=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})^{\top}=(r\cos\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2},r\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2},r\cos\theta_{2})^{\top},

\mathcal{P}(t)\equiv\boldsymbol{0}
follows directly from the spherical symmetry of
\rho\boldsymbol{u}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\rho\boldsymbol{u}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})^{\top}}{r}(r^{2}\rho u)\sin\theta_{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}\theta_{1}\mathrm{d}\theta_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{2}\rho u\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)\int_{0}^{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(\cos\theta_{1}\sin^{2}\theta_{2},\sin\theta_{1}\sin^{2}\theta_{2},\cos\theta_{2}\sin\theta_{2})^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}\theta_{1}\mathrm{d}\theta_{2}=\boldsymbol{0}.

The conservation of total mass can be simply derived by integrating the mass equation
\eqref{eq:1.1}_{1}
over
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
and using the fact that
\rho\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T];W^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
. Finally, Theorem
2.1
(iii) is a directly consequence of Lemmas
4.3
,
4.9
, and
6.5
.

9.
Global Well-Posedness of Regular Solutions with Strictly Positive Initial Density

This section is devoted to establishing the global well-posedness of spherically symmetric classical solutions of the Cauchy problem of system (
1.1
) with general smooth initial data and
strictly positive initial density,
i.e.
,
\inf_{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})>0
so that
\bar{\rho}>0
in
(
1.7
). Certainly, in this case, under the spherical coordinates, the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with (
1.6
)–(
1.7
) in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
for some
T>0
can be written as the following
IBVP
in
[0,T]\times I
:

(9.1)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\rho_{t}+(\rho u)_{r}+\frac{m\rho u}{r}=0,\\[1.0pt]
\displaystyle\rho u_{t}+\rho uu_{r}+A(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}=2a_{1}\delta\big(\rho^{\delta}u_{r}+\frac{m\rho^{\delta}u}{r}\big)_{r}-\frac{2a_{1}m(\rho^{\delta})_{r}u}{r},\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle(\rho,u)|_{t=0}=(\rho_{0},u_{0})\qquad\quad\,\,\,\,\text{for $r\in I$},\\[3.0pt]
\displaystyle u|_{r=0}=0\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T]$},\\[3.0pt]
\displaystyle(\rho,u)\to\left(\bar{\rho},0\right)\qquad\qquad\text{as $r\to\infty\,$ \, for $t\in[0,T]$}.\end{cases}

9.1.
Local Well-Posedness in M-D Coordinates

To establish the local well-posedness when
\inf_{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})>0
, we consider the following reformulated system in M-D coordinates:

(9.2)

\begin{cases}\rho_{t}+\diver(\rho\boldsymbol{u})=0,\\[3.0pt]
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}+\frac{A\gamma}{\gamma-1}\nabla\rho^{\gamma-1}+L\boldsymbol{u}=\frac{\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla\rho^{\delta-1}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}),\end{cases}

where the operators
(L,Q)
are defined in (
2.4
),
i.e.
,

L\boldsymbol{u}=-a_{1}\Delta\boldsymbol{u}-(a_{1}+a_{2})\nabla\diver\boldsymbol{u},\qquad Q(\boldsymbol{u})=2a_{1}D(\boldsymbol{u})+a_{2}\diver\boldsymbol{u}\,\mathbb{I}_{n}.

Notice that the positive lower bound of
\rho
can be obtained via the transport
equation
\eqref{re}_{1}
in some short time. Then, employing the methods used by Matsumura–Nishida
[
43
]
and Sundbye
[
55
]
, we can establish the corresponding local well-posedness results of the regular solutions (as defined in Definition
2.2
) when
\inf_{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})>0
.
We omit the proof here for brevity and give the statement of this theorem:

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
n=2
or
3
,
\bar{\rho}>0
in (
1.7
), and let (
3.8
) hold. If the initial data
(\rho_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})(\boldsymbol{x})
are spherically symmetric and satisfy (
2.15
), then there exists
T_{*}>0
such that the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with (
1.6
)–(
1.7
) admits a unique regular solution
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying (
2.7
), (
2.9
), and (
2.16
) with
T
replaced by
T_{*}
, and

\rho_{tt}\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T_{*}];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})).

Moreover,
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})
is spherically symmetric with form (
2.8
).

9.2.
Global Well-Posedness in Spherically Symmetric Coordinates

The proof for global well-posedness is organized as follows: First, we establish the uniform upper bound of
\rho
. Next, we establish the uniform
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate for the effective velocity, and then obtain the uniform lower bound of
\rho
. Finally, based on these, we obtain global uniform estimates for regular solutions and then show the desired global well-posedness of regular solutions.

This part of the proof can be completed by combining the arguments in §
4
–§
8
with the method presented in
[
13
, Section 10]
. For simplicity, we only list the points that require noticeable modifications in the following three most important parts:

§
9.2.1

Global uniform upper bound of the density;

§
9.2.2

Global uniform bound of the effective velocity;

§
9.2.3

Global uniform lower bound of the density.

In what follows, unless otherwise specified, we adopt all the notations and defined quantities in §
4
–§
6
, and denote
C_{0}\in[1,\infty)
a generic constant depending only on
\left(\bar{\rho},\rho_{0},u_{0},n,a_{1},A,\gamma,\delta\right)
, and
C\left(\nu_{1},\cdots\!,\nu_{k}\right)\in[1,\infty)
a generic constant depending on
C_{0}
and parameters
\left(\nu_{1},\cdots\!,\nu_{k}\right)
, which may be different at each occurrence.

9.2.1.
Global uniform upper bound of
\rho

We can obtain the global uniform upper bound of
\rho
by combining the arguments used in §
4
and
[
13
, Section 10]
.
The major modification lies in the establishment of
L^{p}(I)
-estimates for
(u,v)
, namely, Lemma
4.5
. It should be mentioned that Lemma
4.4
also holds in this case and, for convenience, we present here a modified version of Lemmas
4.2
–
4.3
, which is adapted to the case where
\bar{\rho}>0
in (
1.7
).

Lemma 9.1
.

Assume that
\bar{\rho}>0
in (
1.7
). Then
Lemma
4.2
in
§
4
holds with
\rho^{\gamma}
replaced by
j_{\gamma}(\rho):=(\rho^{\gamma}-\bar{\rho}^{\gamma})-\gamma\bar{\rho}^{\gamma-1}(\rho-\bar{\rho})
and, for any
\omega>0
, there exists
C(\omega)>0
such that

|\chi^{\sharp}_{\omega}\rho(t)|_{\infty}\leq C(\omega)\qquad\text{for any $t\in[0,T]$}.

Now, we give the proof for Lemma
4.5
when
\bar{\rho}>0
in (
1.7
).

Proof of Lemma
4.5
when
\bar{\rho}>0
.

We divide the rest of the proof into two steps.

1.
Following a similar argument in Steps 1–3 of the proof of Lemma
4.7
when
\bar{\rho}=0
, we multiply
\eqref{e1.5hh-}_{2}
by
r^{m}|u|^{p-2}u
, with
p\in[2,\tilde{p}_{m}(\delta))
, and integrate the resulting equality. Then we arrive at

(9.3)

\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+2a_{1}c_{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\rho^{\delta}|u|^{p-2}|\mathrm{D}_{r}u|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq-A\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}|u|^{p-2}u\,\mathrm{d}r:=G^{*}_{1}.

To estimate
G^{*}_{1}
, we introduce the following smooth cut-off functions:

(9.4)

\zeta=\zeta(r)\in C_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}([0,1]),\quad\zeta_{r}\leq 0,\quad\zeta=1\ \ \text{on $[0,\frac{1}{2}]$},\quad\zeta=0\ \ \text{on $[\frac{3}{4},1]$},\quad\zeta^{\sharp}:=1-\zeta.

Then it follows from integration by parts that

(9.5)

\displaystyle G^{*}_{1}

\displaystyle=-A\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta r^{m}(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}|u|^{p-2}u\,\mathrm{d}r-A\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta^{\sharp}r^{m}(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}|u|^{p-2}u\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=A\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}\big((p-1)u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)\,\mathrm{d}r+A\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta_{r}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-2}u\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle-A\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta^{\sharp}r^{m}(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}|u|^{p-2}u\,\mathrm{d}r:=\sum_{i=1}^{3}G^{*}_{1,i}.

For
G^{*}_{1,1}
, we obtain from the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle G^{*}_{1,1}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\mathrm{D}_{r}u\big|_{2}\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\gamma-\frac{\delta}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a_{1}c_{p}}{32}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\mathrm{D}_{r}u\big|_{2}^{2}+C(p)\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{p+m-2}{p\gamma-p\delta+\delta}}\rho\big|_{p\gamma-p\delta+\delta}^{\frac{2p\gamma-2p\delta+2\delta}{p}}\big|(r^{m-2}\rho^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p-2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a_{1}c_{p}}{16}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\mathrm{D}_{r}u\big|_{2}^{2}+C(p)\underline{\big|\chi_{1}^{\flat}r^{\frac{p+m-2}{p\gamma-p\delta+\delta}}\rho\big|_{p\gamma-p\delta+\delta}^{p\gamma-p\delta+\delta}}_{=I_{3,1}},

where
I_{3,1}
is defined in (
4.22
). Hence, following the same argument as in Steps 4.1–4.2 in the proof of Lemma
4.7
when
\bar{\rho}=0
, we obtain that, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

(9.6)

G^{*}_{1,1}\leq\frac{a_{1}c_{p}}{8}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\mathrm{D}_{r}u\big|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma-\delta+1})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}+C(p,\varepsilon).

For
G^{*}_{1,2}
–
G^{*}_{1,3}
, we see from (
4.9
), Lemma
9.1
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle G^{*}_{1,2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}}r^{m}\rho^{\gamma}|u|^{p-1}\,\mathrm{d}r\leq C(p)|\chi_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\sharp}\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-1+\frac{1}{p}}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p-1}\leq C(p)+\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p},

\displaystyle G^{*}_{1,3}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\infty}\!\!r^{m}\rho^{\gamma-\delta+1}|v-u||u|^{p-1}\mathrm{d}r\leq C_{0}|\chi_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\sharp}\rho|_{\infty}^{\gamma-\delta}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}(u,v)\big|_{p}^{p}\leq C_{0}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}(u,v)\big|_{p}^{p}.

Collecting (
9.3
), (
9.5
), and the above, we obtain that, for any
p\in[2,\tilde{p}_{m}(\delta))
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

(9.7)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}u\big|_{p}^{p}+a_{1}c_{p}\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\delta})^{\frac{1}{2}}|u|^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\mathrm{D}_{r}u\big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,\varepsilon)\big(\big|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{p}}(u,v)\big|_{p}^{p}+1\big)+\varepsilon\big|(r^{m}\rho^{\gamma-\delta+1})^{\frac{1}{p}}v\big|_{p}^{p}.

2.
Repeating the same proof of Lemma
4.8
, we can still obtain (
4.32
) when
\bar{\rho}>0
. Therefore, combining (
9.7
) with (
4.32
), choosing
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, and then applying the Grönwall inequality to the resulting inequality, we obtain the desired conclusion.
∎

9.2.2.
Global uniform bound of
v

Once we prove Lemma
5.1
when
\bar{\rho}>0
, the
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimate for
v
can be derived by following the same method in §
5
without further modifications. Hence, we only sketch some revisions to the proof of Lemma
5.1
when
\bar{\rho}>0
.

Proof of Lemma
5.1
when
\bar{\rho}>0
in (
1.7
).

The main difference is in the estimate for the following integral:

\mathrm{G}_{2}^{*}:=-A\int_{0}^{\infty}(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}u\,\mathrm{d}r,

where we have used integration by parts to treat it in the proof of Lemma
5.1
in §
5
; see (
5.3
).

Here, to overcome the estimate away from the origin, we employ the cut-off function
\zeta
in (
9.4
) and rewrite
\mathrm{G}_{2}^{*}
as

|\mathrm{G}_{2}^{*}|\leq A\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}u\,\mathrm{d}r\Big|+A\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta^{\sharp}(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}u\,\mathrm{d}r\Big|.

The first term on the right-hand side of the above can be handled by using integration by parts and the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
in §
5
:

A\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}u\,\mathrm{d}r\Big|\leq C(\varepsilon,T)+\varepsilon|\rho^{\frac{\delta}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}^{2}.

For the second one, it follows from Lemma
9.1
that

\displaystyle A\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}\zeta^{\sharp}(\rho^{\gamma})_{r}u\,\mathrm{d}r\Big|\leq C_{0}\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\infty}\rho^{\gamma-1}|\rho_{r}|u\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}|\chi_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\sharp}r^{-m}|_{\infty}|\chi_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\sharp}\rho|_{\infty}^{\frac{\gamma-\delta}{2}}\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\frac{\gamma+\delta-3}{2}}\rho_{r}\big|_{2}|(r^{m}\rho)^{\frac{1}{2}}u|_{2}\leq C_{0}+\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\frac{\gamma+\delta-3}{2}}\rho_{r}\big|_{2}^{2}.

Collecting the above estimates, we arrive at

|\mathrm{G}_{2}^{*}|\leq C(\varepsilon,T)+\varepsilon|\rho^{\frac{\delta}{2}}u_{r}|_{2}^{2}+\big|r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\frac{\gamma+\delta-3}{2}}\rho_{r}\big|_{2}^{2},

and we can finally close the estimate to complete the proof of Lemma
5.1
when
\bar{\rho}>0
by using the
L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(I))
-estimate of
r^{\frac{m}{2}}\rho^{\frac{\gamma+\delta-3}{2}}\rho_{r}
in Lemma
9.1
.
∎

9.2.3.
Global uniform lower bound of
\rho

Since we already show the global uniform
L^{\infty}(I)
-estimates for
(\rho,v)
, we can follow the same method provided in §
6.1
to obtain Lemmas
6.1
–
6.4
when
\bar{\rho}>0
. Here, we summarize some related estimates for use in Lemma
9.3
below.

Lemma 9.2
.

When
\bar{\rho}>0
in (
1.7
), there exists a constant
C(T)>0
such that

|(\rho,v)(t)|_{\infty}+|u(t)|_{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big(|\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}\mathrm{D}_{r}u|_{2}^{2}+|u|_{\infty}^{4}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T).

Now, based on Lemma
9.2
, we establish the uniform lower bound for
\rho
.

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
\bar{\rho}>0
in (
1.7
).
For any
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times I
,

\rho(t,r)\geq C(T)^{-1},

where
C(T)\geq 1
is a constant depending only on
(T,\bar{\rho},\rho_{0},u_{0},n,a_{1},\delta,\gamma,A)
.

Proof.

Set
\hat{\rho}=\rho/\bar{\rho}
and
\hat{\rho}_{0}=\hat{\rho}|_{t=0}
. First, it follows from (
4.9
) and Lemmas
9.2
and
A.1
that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\log\hat{\rho}|_{\infty}^{3}&\leq C_{0}\big\||\log\hat{\rho}|^{3}\big\|_{1,1}\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(|\log\hat{\rho}|^{3}+|\log\hat{\rho}|^{2}\rho^{1-\delta}(|u|+|v|)\big)\,\mathrm{d}r\\
&\leq C_{0}|\log\hat{\rho}|_{\infty}|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}^{2}+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{1-\delta}|v|_{\infty}|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}^{2}+C_{0}|\rho|_{\infty}^{1-\delta}|\log\hat{\rho}|_{\infty}|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}|u|_{2}\\
&\leq C(T)\big((|\log\hat{\rho}|_{\infty}+1)|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}^{2}+|\log\hat{\rho}|_{\infty}|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}\big),\end{aligned}

which, along with the Young inequality, leads to

(9.8)

|\log\hat{\rho}|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\big(|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}+1\big).

Next, rewrite
\eqref{e1.5hh-}_{1}
as

(\log\hat{\rho})_{t}+u(\log\rho)_{r}+\big(u_{r}+\frac{m}{r}u\big)=0.

Multiplying the above by
\log\hat{\rho}
and integrating over
I
, we obtain from (
4.9
), Lemma
9.2
, and the Hölder and Young inequalities that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq|u|_{2}|(\log\rho)_{r}|_{\infty}|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}+C_{0}|\mathrm{D}_{r}u|_{2}|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\big(|u|_{2}^{2}|\rho|_{\infty}^{2-2\delta}|(u,v)|_{\infty}^{2}+|\rho|_{\infty}^{1-\delta}|\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}\mathrm{D}_{r}u|_{2}^{2}\big)+|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\big(1+|u|_{\infty}^{2}+|\rho^{\frac{\delta-1}{2}}\mathrm{D}_{r}u|_{2}^{2}\big)+|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}^{2},

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, leads to

(9.9)

|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}\leq C(T)\big(|\log\hat{\rho}_{0}|_{2}+1\big).

For the
L^{2}(I)
-boundedness of
\log\hat{\rho}_{0}
, observing that
\rho_{0}(r)\to\bar{\rho}
as
r\to\infty
, one can find a sufficiently large
R_{0}>0
, depending only on
\bar{\rho}
, such that, for all
r\in[R_{0},\infty)
,

\chi_{R_{0}}^{\sharp}|\log\hat{\rho}_{0}|=\chi_{R_{0}}^{\sharp}|\log(1+(\hat{\rho}_{0}-1))|\leq 2\chi_{R_{0}}^{\sharp}|\hat{\rho}_{0}-1|\leq C_{0}\chi_{R_{0}}^{\sharp}|\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho}|.

For such
R_{0}>0
, Lemma
B.1
, together with
0<\inf_{r\in I}\rho_{0}\leq\rho_{0}\leq|\rho_{0}|_{\infty}
, implies

\displaystyle|\log\hat{\rho}_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|\chi_{R_{0}}^{\flat}\log\hat{\rho}_{0}|_{2}+|\chi_{R_{0}}^{\sharp}\log\hat{\rho}_{0}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\sqrt{R_{0}}\big(|\log\rho_{0}|_{\infty}+|\log\bar{\rho}|\big)+C_{0}|\chi_{R_{0}}^{\sharp}(\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho})|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}+C_{0}|r^{\frac{m}{2}}(\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho})|_{2}\leq C_{0}+C_{0}\|\rho_{0}-\bar{\rho}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{0}.

Thus, substituting above into (
9.9
), together with (
9.8
), gives

|\log\hat{\rho}|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\big(|\log\hat{\rho}|_{2}+1\big)\leq C(T)\big(|\log\hat{\rho}_{0}|_{2}+1\big)\leq C(T),

which implies the desired result.
∎

10.
Local Well-Posedness of Regular Solutions with Far-Field Vacuum

This section is devoted to establishing the local well-posedness of regular solutions of the Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with (
1.6
)–(
1.7
) when
\bar{\rho}=0
. We first give the proof of Theorem
3.1
and, at the end of this section, we show that this theorem indeed implies Theorem
3.2
.

10.1.
Linearization

Let
T>0
. In order to solve the nonlinear problem (
3.2
)–(
3.6
), we consider the following linearized problem for
(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta})
in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
(
n=2
,
3
):

(10.1)

\begin{cases}\phi^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t}+\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\phi^{\epsilon,\eta}+(\gamma-1)\phi^{\epsilon,\eta}\diver\boldsymbol{w}=0,\\[3.0pt]
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t}+\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{w}+\nabla\phi^{\epsilon,\eta}+a\sqrt{(h^{\epsilon,\eta})^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}L\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}=\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\epsilon,\eta}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{w}),\\[3.0pt]
h^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t}+\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla h^{\epsilon,\eta}+(\delta-1)g\diver\boldsymbol{w}=0,\\[3.0pt]
(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta})|_{t=0}=(\phi^{\eta}_{0},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0},h^{\eta}_{0})=(\phi_{0}+\eta,\boldsymbol{u}_{0},(\phi_{0}+\eta)^{2\iota})\qquad\text{for $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\[3.0pt]
(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta})\to(\eta,\boldsymbol{0},\eta^{2\iota})\qquad\text{as $|\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty$}\quad\text{for $t\in[0,T]$},\end{cases}

where
\epsilon
and
\eta
are positive constants,

(10.2)

\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\epsilon,\eta}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h^{\epsilon,\eta},

\boldsymbol{w}=(w_{1},\cdots\!,w_{n})^{\top}
is a given spherically symmetric vector function of the form:
\boldsymbol{w}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=w(t,|\boldsymbol{x}|)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{|\boldsymbol{x}|}
,
and
g>0
is a given spherically symmetric function. Moreover,
\boldsymbol{w}
and
g
are independent of (
\epsilon,\eta
) and satisfy

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{w}(0,\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}),\quad g(0,\boldsymbol{x})=h_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}),\quad g\in L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}),

\displaystyle\nabla g\in C([0,T];D^{1,n}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad g_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle\nabla g_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad\boldsymbol{w}\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];H^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{w}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad t^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})).

For the sake of clarity, we declare that the functions
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})
shown above in problem (
10.1
) are exactly the ones in (
3.5
).

It follows from the classical theory
[
21
,
36
,
42
]
, at least when
\eta

and
\epsilon
are positive, that the following global well-posedness of (
10.1
) in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
holds.

Lemma 10.1
.

Let (
3.8
) hold,
\epsilon>0
, and
\eta>0
.
If the initial data
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})(\boldsymbol{x})
are spherically symmetric and satisfy (
3.9
)
–
(
3.10
), then, for any
T>0
, there exists a unique classical solution
(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta})
in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
of the Cauchy problem
(
10.1
)
such that

\displaystyle\inf_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi(t,x)>0,\quad(\nabla\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\phi^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t})\in C([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle h^{\epsilon,\eta}\in L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap C([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}),\quad\nabla h^{\epsilon,\eta}\in C([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

(10.3)

\displaystyle h^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}\in C([0,T];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];H^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad t^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{tt}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})).

Moreover,
(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\epsilon,\eta})
is spherically symmetric and takes the form

(10.4)

\displaystyle(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta})(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\phi^{\epsilon,\eta})(t,|\boldsymbol{x}|),\quad(\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\epsilon,\eta})(t,\boldsymbol{x})=(u^{\epsilon,\eta},\psi^{\epsilon,\eta})(t,|\boldsymbol{x}|)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{|\boldsymbol{x}|},

where
\psi^{\epsilon,\eta}(t,r)=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}(h^{\epsilon,\eta})_{r}(t,r)
.

Proof.

The well-posedness follows from the classical theories for the transport equation and the parabolic equations when
\eta

and
\epsilon
are positive, which can be found in
[
36
,
42
]
. We only show that the solution
(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\epsilon,\eta})
is spherically symmetric and takes form (
10.4
). For simplicity, in the rest of the proof, we drop the superscript
\epsilon
and
\eta
in
(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\epsilon,\eta})
, and let
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},h)
be the unique solution of (
10.1
) in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
(\boldsymbol{\psi},h)
satisfy (
10.2
).

Indeed, since
(\phi_{0}^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\eta},h_{0}^{\eta})
is spherically symmetric, then

\phi_{0}^{\eta}(\boldsymbol{x})=\phi_{0}^{\eta}(\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}),\ \ \boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\eta}(\boldsymbol{x})=\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\eta}(\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}),\ \ h_{0}^{\eta}(\boldsymbol{x})=h_{0}^{\eta}(\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})\qquad\text{for any $\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)$}.

Thus, denoting

\hat{\phi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\phi(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}),\quad\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}),\quad\hat{h}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=h(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}),

we obtain from
|\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}|=|\boldsymbol{x}|
and (
1.7
) that

\displaystyle(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{h})|_{t=0}=(\hat{\phi}_{0},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{0},\hat{h}_{0})=(\phi_{0}^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\eta},h_{0}^{\eta}),

\displaystyle\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}(t,\boldsymbol{x})|_{|\boldsymbol{x}|=0}=\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u}(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})|_{|\boldsymbol{x}|=0}=\boldsymbol{0}\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T]$},

\displaystyle(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{h})\to(\eta,\boldsymbol{0},\eta^{2\iota})\qquad\text{as $|\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty$}\hskip 19.91692pt\text{for $t\in[0,T]$}.

Next, we show that
(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{h})
is also a solution of (
10.1
). For convenience, in what follows, we adopt the Einstein summation convention that
any index that appears exactly twice in a term is summed over.
For any
\mathcal{O}=(\mathcal{O}_{lk})_{n\times n}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
, let
\boldsymbol{x}=(x_{1},\cdots\!,x_{n})^{\top}
and
\boldsymbol{y}=(y_{1},\cdots\!,y_{n})^{\top}
satisfying
\boldsymbol{y}=\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}
. Clearly,
y_{l}=\mathcal{O}_{lk}x_{k}
,
\mathcal{O}_{ki}\mathcal{O}_{kj}=\delta_{ij}
, and
\boldsymbol{w}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{w}(t,\boldsymbol{y})
.

To show that
\hat{\phi}
satisfies
\eqref{ln}_{1}
, by the following direct calculations:

\displaystyle(\hat{\phi}_{t}+\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\hat{\phi})(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\phi_{t}+\mathcal{O}_{ki}\mathcal{O}_{li}w_{l}\partial_{y_{k}}\phi)(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\phi_{t}+\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla_{\boldsymbol{y}}\phi)(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

\displaystyle(\hat{\phi}\diver\boldsymbol{w})(t,\boldsymbol{x})

\displaystyle=(\phi\mathcal{O}_{kj}\mathcal{O}_{lj}\partial_{y_{k}}w_{l})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\phi\diver_{\boldsymbol{y}}\boldsymbol{w})(t,\boldsymbol{y}),

we find that
\hat{\phi}
satisfies
\eqref{ln}_{1}
indeed. Clearly, repeating the same calculation, we can also show that
\hat{h}
satisfies
\eqref{ln}_{3}
.

Next, we show that
(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{h},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})
satisfies
\eqref{ln}_{2}
.
Notice that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}_{t}+\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{w})_{i}(t,\boldsymbol{x})&=(\mathcal{O}_{qi}(\partial_{t}u_{q}+\mathcal{O}_{lj}\mathcal{O}_{kj}w_{l}\partial_{y_{k}}w_{q}))(t,\boldsymbol{y})\\
&=(\mathcal{O}_{qi}(\partial_{t}u_{q}+w_{k}\partial_{y_{k}}w_{q}))(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla_{\boldsymbol{y}}\boldsymbol{w}))_{i}(t,\boldsymbol{y}),\\
\partial_{i}\hat{\phi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})&=(\mathcal{O}_{ki}\partial_{y_{k}}\phi)(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{y}}\phi)_{i}(t,\boldsymbol{y}),\\
\Delta\hat{u}_{i}(t,\boldsymbol{x})&=(\mathcal{O}_{lj}\mathcal{O}_{kj}\mathcal{O}_{qi}\partial_{y_{l}y_{k}}u_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\Delta_{\boldsymbol{y}}\boldsymbol{u})_{i}(t,\boldsymbol{y}),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\begin{aligned} \partial_{i}\diver\hat{\boldsymbol{u}}(t,\boldsymbol{x})&=(\mathcal{O}_{li}\mathcal{O}_{kj}\mathcal{O}_{qj}\partial_{y_{l}y_{k}}u_{q})(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\nabla_{\boldsymbol{y}}\diver_{\boldsymbol{y}}\boldsymbol{u})_{i}(t,\boldsymbol{y}),\\
2(\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\cdot D(\boldsymbol{w}))_{i}(t,\boldsymbol{x})&=(\mathcal{O}_{kj}\psi_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{lj}\mathcal{O}_{pi}\partial_{y_{l}}w_{p}+\mathcal{O}_{li}\mathcal{O}_{pj}\partial_{y_{l}}w_{p}))(t,\boldsymbol{y})\\
&=(\mathcal{O}_{li}\psi_{k}(\partial_{y_{k}}w_{l}+\partial_{y_{l}}w_{k}))(t,\boldsymbol{y})=2(\mathcal{O}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot D_{\boldsymbol{y}}(\boldsymbol{w})))_{i}(t,\boldsymbol{y}),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\quad\ \ \,\,\,\,\begin{aligned} (\hat{\psi}_{i}\diver\boldsymbol{w})(t,\boldsymbol{x})&=(\mathcal{O}_{kj}\psi_{k}(\mathcal{O}_{li}\mathcal{O}_{pi}\partial_{y_{l}}w_{p}))(t,\boldsymbol{y})=(\mathcal{O}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\psi}\diver_{\boldsymbol{y}}\boldsymbol{w}))_{i}(t,\boldsymbol{y}).\end{aligned}

Hence, the above identities yield that
(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{h},\hat{\boldsymbol{\psi}})
satisfies
\eqref{ln}_{2}
.

Now, since we have shown that
(\hat{\phi},\hat{\boldsymbol{u}},\hat{h})
is also a solution of the linearized problem (
10.1
), which takes the initial data
(\phi_{0}^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{\eta},h_{0}^{\eta})
, the uniqueness of the solution implies

\phi(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})=\phi(t,\boldsymbol{x}),\qquad(\mathcal{O}^{\top}\boldsymbol{u})(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{x}),\qquad h(t,\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})=h(t,\boldsymbol{x}).

Therefore,
(\phi,h)(t,\boldsymbol{x})=(\phi,h)(t,|\boldsymbol{x}|)
with some functions
(\phi,h)(t,r)
defined on
[0,T]\times I
. Moreover, it follows from Lemma
A.7
in Appendix
A
that
\boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=u(t,|\boldsymbol{x}|)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{|\boldsymbol{x}|}
with some function
u(t,r)
defined on
[0,T]\times I
, and
\boldsymbol{u}(t,\boldsymbol{x})|_{|\boldsymbol{x}|=0}=\boldsymbol{0}
. Finally, the spherical symmetry of
\boldsymbol{\psi}
follows directly from that of
h
and (
10.2
).
∎

Now we are going to derive the uniform
a priori
estimates, independent of
(\epsilon,\eta)
, for solutions
(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta})
of
(
10.1
)
obtained in Lemma
10.1
. In the rest of §
10
,
C\geq 4
denotes a generic constant depending only on
(A,a_{1},a_{2},\gamma,\delta,T)
and may be different at each occurrence.

10.2.
A Priori
Estimates Independent of
(\epsilon,\eta)

Assume that
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})
satisfy (
3.9
)–(
3.10
) and
(\phi^{\eta}_{0},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0})
are given in
\eqref{ln}_{4}
.

First, it follows from Lemmas
A.1
,
A.10
, and
C.2
(in Appendix
C
), the facts that
\delta>1-\frac{1}{n}
and
\phi_{0}\rightarrow 0
as
|\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty
,
and the initial assumption (
3.9
) on
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0})
(or the equivalent one (
2.5
) on
(\rho_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})
) that

(\phi_{0},\ \boldsymbol{\psi_{0}}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla\phi^{2\iota}_{0},\ \nabla\phi^{\iota}_{0})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\ \ \text{for $n=2,3$},\qquad\phi_{0}\in L^{6}(\mathbb{R}^{3}).

Next, for any fixed
\eta\in(0,1]
, denote

(10.5)

\mathcal{G}^{\eta}_{1}=(\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{\iota}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0},\qquad\mathcal{G}^{\eta}_{2}=(\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{\iota}\nabla((\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{2\iota}L\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0}),\qquad\boldsymbol{g}^{\eta}_{*}=(\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{2\iota}L\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0}.

Since

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\big(\|\phi_{0}\|_{L^{n^{*}}}\|\phi_{0}\|^{-\iota}_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{n}}+\|\nabla\phi^{\iota}_{0}\|_{L^{2n}}\|\nabla\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{n-1}}}\big),\\
\|(\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{\iota}\nabla^{2}\phi^{\eta}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}&=\big\|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}\frac{\phi_{0}^{-\iota}}{(\phi_{0}+\eta)^{-\iota}}\big\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\phi_{0}^{\iota}\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}},\end{aligned}

(10.6)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98857pt\begin{aligned} \|\mathcal{G}^{\eta}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}&=\big\|\frac{\phi_{0}^{-\iota}}{(\phi_{0}+\eta)^{-\iota}}\mathcal{G}_{1}\big\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\mathcal{G}_{1}\|_{L^{2}},\\
\|\mathcal{G}^{\eta}_{2}\|_{L^{2}}&=\big\|\frac{\phi_{0}^{-3\iota}}{(\phi_{0}+\eta)^{-3\iota}}(\mathcal{G}_{2}-\frac{\eta\nabla\phi_{0}^{2\iota}}{\phi_{0}+\eta}\phi_{0}^{\iota}Lu_{0})\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq C(\|\mathcal{G}_{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\phi_{0}\|^{-\iota}_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{g}^{\eta}_{*}\|_{L^{2}}),\\
\|\boldsymbol{g}_{*}^{\eta}\|_{L^{2}}&=\big\|\frac{\phi_{0}^{-2\iota}}{(\phi_{0}+\eta)^{-2\iota}}\boldsymbol{g}_{*}\big\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\boldsymbol{g}_{*}\|_{L^{2}},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\hskip 8.53581pt\begin{aligned} \|\nabla^{2}(\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{2\iota}\|_{L^{n}}&\leq C\big\|\nabla\big(\frac{\phi_{0}^{-2\iota+1}}{(\phi_{0}+\eta)^{-2\iota+1}}\nabla\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\big)\big\|_{L^{n}}\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{n}}+\|\nabla\phi^{\iota}_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2n}}\big),\\
\|\nabla^{3}(\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{2\iota}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\big\|\nabla^{2}\big(\frac{\phi_{0}^{-2\iota+1}}{(\phi_{0}+\eta)^{-2\iota+1}}\nabla\phi_{0}^{2\iota}\big)\big\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq C\big(\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\phi_{0}\|^{-\iota}_{L^{\infty}}(\delta_{3n}\|\nabla\phi^{\iota}_{0}\|^{3}_{L^{2n}}+\delta_{2n}\|\nabla\phi^{\iota}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\phi^{\iota}_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2n}})\big)\\
&\quad+C\|\phi_{0}\|^{-\iota}_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\phi^{\iota}_{0}\|_{L^{n^{*}}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{n}},\end{aligned}

we can check that there exists a constant
c_{0}\geq 1
independent of
\eta
such that

(10.7)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\phi^{\eta}_{0}\|_{H^{2}}+\|\phi^{\eta}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\delta_{3n}\|\phi^{\eta}_{0}-\eta\|_{L^{6}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0}\|_{H^{3}}+\|\nabla h^{\eta}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap D^{1,n}\cap D^{2}}+\|\nabla(h^{\eta}_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|_{L^{2n}}

\displaystyle+\|(\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{\iota}\nabla^{2}\phi^{\eta}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|(h^{\eta}_{0})^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathcal{G}^{\eta}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathcal{G}^{\eta}_{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{g}^{\eta}_{*}\|_{L^{2}}+2+\eta\leq c_{0}.

Remark 10.1
.

Based on (
10.7
), we also have

(10.8)

\displaystyle\|(\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{2\iota}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0}\|_{D^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\boldsymbol{g}^{\eta}_{*}\|_{L^{2}}+\|G(\psi^{\eta}_{0},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0})\|_{L^{2}})\leq Cc^{2}_{0},

\displaystyle\|(\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|(\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{2\iota}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0}\|_{D^{2}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}_{0}\|_{L^{n}}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0}\|_{L^{n^{*}}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0}\|_{L^{2}})\leq Cc^{2}_{0}.

Indeed, it follows from the definition of
\boldsymbol{g}^{\eta}_{*}
,
\phi^{\eta}_{0}>\eta
, and
\eqref{ln}_{5}
that

L((\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{2\iota}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0})=\boldsymbol{g}^{\eta}_{*}-\frac{\delta-1}{a\delta}G(\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}_{0},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0}),\qquad\text{with \ $(\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{2\iota}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0}\to\boldsymbol{0}$ as $|\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty$},

where
\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}_{0}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla(\phi^{\eta}_{0})^{2\iota}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h^{\eta}_{0}
and

(10.9)

G(\boldsymbol{f}_{1},\boldsymbol{f}_{2}):=a_{1}\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{f}_{2}+a_{1}\diver(\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\otimes\boldsymbol{f}_{1})+(a_{1}+a_{2})(\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\,\diver\boldsymbol{f}_{2}+\boldsymbol{f}_{1}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{f}_{2}+\boldsymbol{f}_{2}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{f}_{1}).

Then (
10.8
) follows directly from (
10.7
) and the classical elliptic theory in
Lemma
A.12
.

Now let
T>0
be a fixed constant, and assume that there exist
T^{*}\in(0,T]
and constants
c_{i}\ (i=1,\cdots\!,5)
such that

1<c_{0}\leq c_{1}\leq c_{2}\leq c_{3}\leq c_{4}\leq c_{5},

and

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}\|\nabla g\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}\cap D^{1,n}\cap D^{2}}\leq c_{1}^{2},\ \ 0<g^{-1}\leq c_{1},\ \ \sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}\big(\|g_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}+\|g^{-1}g_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\big)\leq c^{2}_{4},

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}\|\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\int^{T^{*}}_{0}\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)+\int^{T^{*}}_{0}\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{D^{3}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c_{3}^{2},

(10.10)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{D^{3}}+\|\sqrt{g}\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)+\int^{T^{*}}_{0}\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{D^{4}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c_{4}^{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}\|g\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}+\int^{T^{*}}_{0}\big(\|(g\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w})_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|g\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c_{4}^{2},

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}\big(t\|\boldsymbol{w}\|^{2}_{D^{4}}+t\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+t\|g\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)+\int^{T^{*}}_{0}\|g_{tt}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c^{2}_{5},

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T^{*}]}t\|\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int^{T^{*}}_{0}t\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}+\|\sqrt{g}\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{3}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c_{5}^{2}.

Here,
T^{*}
and
c_{i}

(i=1,\cdots\!,5)
will be determined later, and depend only on
c_{0}
and the fixed constants
(A,a_{1},a_{2},\gamma,\delta,T)
.

In the rest of §
10.2
, without causing ambiguity,
we simply drop superscripts
\epsilon
and
\eta
in

(\phi^{\eta}_{0},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0},h^{\eta}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}_{0})
,

(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\epsilon,\eta})
, and
(\mathcal{G}^{\eta}_{1},\mathcal{G}^{\eta}_{2},\boldsymbol{g}_{*}^{\eta})
, and we let
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},h)
be the unique classical solution of
(
10.1
)
in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
obtained in Lemma
10.1
.

10.2.1.
The estimates for
\phi
.

First, we give the estimates for
\phi
.

Lemma 10.2
.

For any
0\leq t\leq T_{1}:=\min\{T^{*},(1+Cc_{4})^{-6}\}
,

\displaystyle\|\phi(t)-\eta\|_{L^{\infty}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{3}}\leq Cc_{0},\qquad\|\phi_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}c_{2},\qquad\|\phi_{t}(t)\|_{D^{1}}\leq Cc_{0}c_{3},

\displaystyle\|\phi_{t}(t)\|_{D^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}c_{4},\qquad\|\phi_{tt}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{4}^{3},\qquad\int^{t}_{0}\|\phi_{tt}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{0}^{2}c_{4}^{2}.

Proof.

First, let
X(t;\boldsymbol{x})
be the particle path defined by

(10.11)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}X(t;\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{w}(t,X(t;\boldsymbol{x}))\ \ \text{for $t\in[0,T]$},\qquad\text{with $X(0;\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$}.

Then, integrating
\eqref{ln}_{1}
along
X(t;\boldsymbol{x})
, we see that,
for
0\leq t\leq T_{1}:=\min\{T^{*},(1+Cc_{4})^{-6}\}
,

\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\phi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}e^{\int_{0}^{t}C\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}s}\leq Cc_{0}.

Next, it follows from the energy estimates for the transport equation
and (
10.10
) that

\|\phi-\eta\|_{D^{1}\cap D^{3}}\leq C\Big(\|\phi_{0}-\eta\|_{D^{1}\cap D^{3}}+\eta\int^{t}_{0}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\exp\Big(\int^{t}_{0}C\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{4}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\leq Cc_{0}

for
t\in[0,T_{1}]
, which, together with
\eqref{ln}_{1}
and (
10.10
), yields the desired estimates.
∎

10.2.2.
The estimates for
\boldsymbol{\psi}
.

It follows from
\boldsymbol{\psi}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h
and
\eqref{ln}_{3}
that

(10.12)

\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}+\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}+B^{*}\boldsymbol{\psi}+a\delta(g\nabla\diver\boldsymbol{w}+\nabla g\diver\boldsymbol{w})=0,

where
B^{*}=B^{*}(\boldsymbol{w})=(\nabla\boldsymbol{w})^{\top}
. Now we give the estimates for
\boldsymbol{\psi}
.

Lemma 10.3
.

For any
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

\|\boldsymbol{\psi}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}\cap D^{1,n}\cap D^{2}}\leq Cc_{0}^{2},\quad\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{3}^{2},\quad\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}(t)\|^{2}_{D^{1}}+\int^{t}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{4}^{4}.

Proof.

First, integrating (
10.12
) along
X(t;\boldsymbol{x})
(see (
10.11
)), we obtain that, for
t\in[0,T_{1}]
,

(10.13)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|g\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla g\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)e^{\int_{0}^{t}C\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}s}

\displaystyle\leq C(c_{0}+c_{4}t^{\frac{1}{2}}+c_{1}c_{4}t)e^{Cc_{4}t}\leq Cc_{0},

where the
L^{\infty}
-boundedness for
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}
is due to
\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\in D^{1,n}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and Lemma
C.2
.

Next, let
\boldsymbol{\varsigma}=(\varsigma_{1},\cdots\!,\varsigma_{n})
be the multi-index. Applying
n|\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\boldsymbol{\psi}|^{n-2}\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\boldsymbol{\psi}\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}
(
|\boldsymbol{\varsigma}|=1
) and
2\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\boldsymbol{\psi}\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}
(
|\boldsymbol{\varsigma}|=2
) to (
10.12
) respectively, and then integrating these two resulting equalities over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we obtain

(10.14)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|^{n}_{L^{n}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|^{n}_{L^{n}}+C\|\Theta_{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\|_{L^{n}}\|\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|^{n-1}_{L^{n}}

\displaystyle\text{with $|\boldsymbol{\varsigma}|=1$},

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\|\Theta_{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\text{with $|\boldsymbol{\varsigma}|=2$},

where

\displaystyle\Theta_{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}=\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(B^{*}\boldsymbol{\psi})-B^{*}\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\boldsymbol{\psi}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\big(\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}(\boldsymbol{w}_{k}\partial_{k}\boldsymbol{\psi})-\boldsymbol{w}_{k}\partial_{k}\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\boldsymbol{\psi}\big)+a\delta\partial_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\big(g\nabla\diver\boldsymbol{w}+\nabla g\diver\boldsymbol{w}\big).

Then we can directly check that, for
|\boldsymbol{\varsigma}|=1
,

(10.15)

\|\Theta_{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\|_{L^{n}}\leq C\big(\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{n}}\|(\boldsymbol{\psi},\nabla g)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|(\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi},\nabla^{2}g)\|_{L^{n}}+\|\nabla(g\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w})\|_{L^{n}}\big),

and, for
|\boldsymbol{\varsigma}|=2
,

(10.16)

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi},\nabla^{3}g)\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{6}}\|(\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi},\nabla^{2}g)\|_{L^{3}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\|(\boldsymbol{\psi},\nabla g)\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\|g\nabla\diver\boldsymbol{w}\|_{D^{2}}\big).

Hence, it follows from
(
10.14
)
–
(
10.16
)
and the Grönwall inequality that

\|\boldsymbol{\psi}(t)\|_{D^{1,n}\cap D^{2}}\leq\Big(c_{0}+Cc^{2}_{4}t+C\int_{0}^{t}\|g\nabla\diver\boldsymbol{w}\|_{D^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)e^{Cc_{4}t}\leq Cc_{0}\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T_{1}]$},

which, along with (
10.10
) and (
10.12
)–(
10.13
), implies

\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc^{2}_{3},\quad\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}(t)\|_{D^{1}}\leq Cc^{2}_{4},\quad\int_{0}^{t}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc^{4}_{4}\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T_{1}]$}.

∎

10.2.3.
A priori
estimates for auxiliary variables related to
h

We define the quantities:

(\varphi,\varphi_{0})=(h^{-1},h_{0}^{-1}).

Lemma 10.4
.

For any
t\in[0,T_{1}]
and
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle C^{-1}\leq(gh^{-1})(t,\boldsymbol{x})\leq C,\quad h(t,\boldsymbol{x})\geq(2c_{0})^{-1},\quad\|\varphi(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\sqrt{h}(t)\|_{L^{2n}}\leq Cc_{0},

\displaystyle\|h^{-1}h_{t}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\leq Cc_{2}^{\frac{9}{2}}c^{\frac{3}{2}}_{4},\qquad\int^{t}_{0}\|h^{-1}h_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2n}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{4}^{2}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps.

1.
Set
\Lambda=\Lambda(t,\boldsymbol{x})=gh^{-1}
. We then see from
\eqref{ln}_{3}
that
\Lambda^{-1}
satisfies

(\Lambda^{-1})_{t}+(g^{-1}g_{t})\Lambda^{-1}=-(g^{-1}\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla h+(\delta-1)\diver\boldsymbol{w}),\qquad\Lambda^{-1}(0,\boldsymbol{x})=1.

Hence, the standard argument for ODE gives that, for
(t,\boldsymbol{x})\in[0,T_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\Lambda^{-1}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=e^{-\int_{0}^{t}(g^{-1}g_{t})(\tau,\boldsymbol{x})\mathrm{d}\tau}\Big(1-\!\int_{0}^{t}\!\big(g^{-1}\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla h+(\delta-1)\diver\boldsymbol{w}\big)(\tau,\boldsymbol{x})e^{\int_{0}^{\tau}(g^{-1}g_{t})(s,\boldsymbol{x})\mathrm{d}s}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big),

which, along with Lemma
10.3
and (
10.10
), yields

(10.17)

\Lambda^{-1}\in[C^{-1},C]\iff\Lambda\in[C^{-1},C].

2.
Note that

\varphi_{t}+\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\varphi-(\delta-1)\Lambda\varphi\diver\boldsymbol{w}=0.

Then the standard argument for ODE implies that, for
(t,\boldsymbol{x})\in[0,T_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

(10.18)

\frac{2}{3}\eta^{-2\iota}<\varphi(t,\boldsymbol{x})<2\|\varphi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 2c_{0}\implies h(t,\boldsymbol{x})>(2c_{0})^{-1}.

3.
It follows from
\eqref{ln}_{3}
that

(\sqrt{h})_{t}+\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\sqrt{h}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{h}}(\delta-1)g\diver\boldsymbol{w}=0.

Applying
\nabla
to the above,
multiplying by
2n|\nabla\sqrt{h}|^{2n-2}\nabla\sqrt{h}
,
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
yield

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\nabla\sqrt{h}\|_{L^{2n}}\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\sqrt{h}\|_{L^{2n}}+C\big\|\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\big(\|g\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2n}}+\|\nabla g\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2n}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\|gh^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\|\diver\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2n}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}},

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, (
10.10
), (
10.18
), and Lemma
10.3
, leads to

\|\nabla\sqrt{h}(t)\|_{L^{2n}}\leq Cc_{0}\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T_{1}]$}.

4.

Finally, it follows from
\eqref{ln}_{3}
, (
10.10
), (
10.17
)–(
10.18
), and Lemmas
10.3
and
A.2
that

\displaystyle\|h^{-1}h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|gh^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\mathrm{div}\,\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(c^{3}_{2}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|^{\frac{4-n}{4}}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{w}\|^{\frac{n}{4}}_{L^{2}}\big)\leq Cc^{2}_{2}c^{\frac{4-n}{4}}_{2}c^{\frac{n}{4}}_{4},

\displaystyle\|h^{-1}h_{tt}\|_{L^{2n}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2n}}+\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{2n}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\|gh^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big(\|g^{-1}g_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2n}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2n}}\big)\leq C\big(c^{4}_{4}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big),

which, along with (
10.10
), yields the rest of estimates.
∎

10.2.4.
The estimates for
\boldsymbol{u}

Now we derive the estimates for
\boldsymbol{u}
in the following several lemmas.
For simplicity, we denote

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle:=-\boldsymbol{u}_{t}-\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{w}-\nabla\phi+\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{w}),\quad\mathcal{H}_{0}:=\mathcal{H}+\boldsymbol{u}_{t},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{H}-aG(\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}},\boldsymbol{u}),\quad\mathcal{H}_{2}:=\mathcal{H}_{t}-a\frac{h}{\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}}L\boldsymbol{u}h_{t}-aG(\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}},\boldsymbol{u}_{t}),

where
G(\cdot,\cdot)
is defined by (
10.9
).

Lemma 10.5
.

For any
t\in[0,T_{1}]
, we have the following estimates
:

(i)

Estimates on
\mathcal{H}
:

\|\mathcal{H}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|u_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big),\ \ \|\mathcal{H}\|_{D^{1}}\leq C\big(\|u_{t}\|_{D^{1}}+c_{3}^{2}\big),\ \ \|\mathcal{H}\|_{D^{2}}\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{2}}+c_{4}^{2}\big);

(ii)

Estimates on
\mathcal{H}_{0}
:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{H}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}},\qquad\|(\mathcal{H}_{0})_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc^{3}_{4},

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{H}_{0})_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(c^{3}_{4}+c_{4}^{2}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{4}\|\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\nabla\phi_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{4}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\big);

(iii)

Estimates on
\mathcal{H}_{1}
:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{H}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{0}^{3}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big),

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{H}_{1}\|_{D^{1}}\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{1}}+c^{4}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{H^{2}}+c_{3}^{2}\big);

(iv)

Estimates on
\mathcal{H}_{2}
:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{H}_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+c^{3}_{4}\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+c^{3}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+c^{3}_{4}\big),

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{H}_{2}\|_{D^{1}}\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{D^{1}}+c_{0}^{4}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{H^{2}}+c_{4}^{4}\big(\|h\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{H^{1}}\big)+c_{3}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{4}^{3}\big).

Proof.

It follows from the Hölder inequality, (
10.10
), and Lemmas
10.2
–
10.4
and
A.2
that

(10.19)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{6}}\leq C\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{H^{1}}\leq Cc_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\sqrt{h}\|^{2}_{L^{6}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{3}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\delta_{3n}\|\nabla\sqrt{h}\|^{2}_{L^{6}}\!+\delta_{2n}\|\nabla\sqrt{h}\|^{\frac{4}{3}}_{L^{4}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|^{\frac{2}{3}}_{L^{\infty}}\|\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{3}}_{L^{\infty}}\!+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{3}}\big)\leq Cc^{3}_{0},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{3}\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\delta_{3n}\|\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\sqrt{h}\|^{3}_{L^{2n}}+\delta_{2n}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\sqrt{h}\|^{2}_{L^{2n}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{n}}\|\nabla\sqrt{h}\|_{L^{n^{*}}}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq Cc^{4}_{0}.

Then, based on (
10.19
), we obtain from the Hölder inequality, (
10.10
), and Lemmas
10.2
–
10.4
,
A.1
–
A.2
, and
C.1
–
C.2
that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\mathcal{H}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{6}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{3}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq Cc_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}},\\
\|(\mathcal{H}_{0})_{t}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\phi_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq Cc^{3}_{4},\\
\|(\mathcal{H}_{0})_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{6}}\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{3}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\\
&\quad+C\big(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{3}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{6}}\big)\\
&\leq C\big(c^{3}_{4}+c_{4}^{2}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{4}\|\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\nabla\phi_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{4}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\big),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49428pt\begin{aligned} \|\mathcal{H}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\big(\|\mathcal{H}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big),\\
\|\mathcal{H}\|_{D^{1}}&\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1,n}}\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{2}}\big)\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{1}}+c_{3}^{2}\big),\\
\|\mathcal{H}\|_{D^{2}}&\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{3}}^{2}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{H^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{D^{1,n}\cap D^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{3}}\big)\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{2}}+c_{4}^{2}\big),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\hskip 15.649pt\begin{aligned} \|\mathcal{H}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\big(\|\mathcal{H}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{3}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{6}}\big)\\
&\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{0}^{3}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{0}^{\frac{7}{2}}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{2}^{\frac{3}{2}}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{aligned} \|\mathcal{H}_{1}\|_{D^{1}}&\leq C\big(\|\mathcal{H}\|_{D^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\big)+C\|\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{D^{1,3}\cap D^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{H^{2}}\\
&\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{1}}+c^{4}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{H^{2}}+c_{3}^{2}\big),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\hskip 15.649pt\begin{aligned} \|\mathcal{H}_{2}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\big(\|((\mathcal{H}_{0})_{t},\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})\|_{L^{2}}+\|h^{-1}h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\\
&\quad+C\big(\|\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{3}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{6}}\big)\\
&\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+c^{3}_{4}\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+c^{3}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+c^{3}_{4}\big),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{aligned} \|\mathcal{H}_{2}\|_{D^{1}}&\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{D^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{3}}\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\phi_{t}\|_{H^{2}}\big)\\
&\quad+C\big(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap D^{1,3}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{H^{1}}\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{H^{3}}+\|h^{-1}h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\\
&\quad+C\big(\|h^{-1}h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|_{L^{3}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{6}}\big)\\
&\quad+C\big(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{D^{1,3}\cap D^{2}}\big)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{H^{2}}\\
&\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{D^{1}}+c_{0}^{4}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{H^{2}}+c_{4}^{4}\big(\|h\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{H^{1}}\big)+c_{3}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{4}^{3}\big).\end{aligned}

∎

Lemma 10.6
.

Let
T_{2}:=\min\{T_{1},(1+Cc_{4})^{-20}\}
.
Then, for any
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int^{t}_{0}\big(\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Cc^{4}_{0},

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|^{2}_{D^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{2}^{10}c_{3},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\big(\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|h\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Cc^{6}_{0}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate on
\boldsymbol{u}
.

Multiplying
\eqref{ln}_{2}
by
\boldsymbol{u}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we obtain from the Hölder inequality and the Young inequality that

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+aa_{1}\|(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+a(a_{1}+a_{2})\|(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}\diver\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\mathcal{H}_{0}-a\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u})\big)\cdot\boldsymbol{u}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C\big(\|\mathcal{H}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(c_{0}^{3}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+c_{3}^{4}\big)+\frac{1}{2}aa_{1}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, yields

(10.20)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int^{t}_{0}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{3}^{4}t\big)e^{Cc_{0}^{3}t}\leq Cc^{2}_{0}\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T_{2}]$}.

2.
D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate on
\boldsymbol{u}
.
Multiplying
\eqref{ln}_{2}
by
\boldsymbol{u}_{t}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we obtain from the Hölder and Young inequalities, and Lemmas
10.2
–
10.4
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(aa_{1}\|(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+a(a_{1}+a_{2})\|(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}\diver\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\big(\mathcal{H}_{0}-a\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u})\big)\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{a}{2}\frac{hh_{t}}{\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}}\big(a_{1}|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\diver\boldsymbol{u}|^{2}\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\mathcal{H}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|h_{t}h^{-1}\|_{\infty}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(c_{4}^{3}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c_{3}^{4}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

which, along with the Grönwall inequality and (
10.7
), implies that, for
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(10.21)

\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int^{t}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\big(c^{2}_{0}+c_{3}^{4}t\big)e^{Cc_{4}^{3}t}\leq Cc^{2}_{0},\qquad\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq Cc^{3}_{0}.

By the definitions of the Lamé operator
L
and
\boldsymbol{\psi}
and equation
\eqref{ln}_{2}
, we have

(10.22)
