aL(\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\boldsymbol{u})=a\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}L\boldsymbol{u}-aG(\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}},\boldsymbol{u})=\mathcal{H}_{1}.

Then it follows from (
10.19
)–(
10.21
), and Lemmas
A.12
and
10.3
–
10.5
that

(10.23)

\displaystyle\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{D^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{3}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{6}}+\|\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\mathcal{H}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{2}^{4}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{2}^{4}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big),

which, along with (
10.21
) and Lemma
10.4
, implies

\int_{0}^{T_{2}}\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc^{2}_{0},\qquad\int_{0}^{T_{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc^{4}_{0}.

3.
D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimate on
\boldsymbol{u}
.
Applying
\partial_{t}
to
\eqref{ln}_{2}
yields

(10.24)

\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}+a\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}L\boldsymbol{u}_{t}=(\mathcal{H}_{0})_{t}-a\frac{h}{\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}}h_{t}L\boldsymbol{u}.

Multiplying (
10.24
) by
\boldsymbol{u}_{t}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
lead to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+aa_{1}\|(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+a(a_{1}+a_{2})\|(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}\diver\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\mathcal{H}_{0})_{t}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}a\frac{h}{\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}}h_{t}L\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}a\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}_{t})\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|(\mathcal{H}_{0})_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\|h^{-1}h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(c^{6}_{4}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+c^{9}_{4}\big)+\frac{aa_{1}}{2}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Integrating the above over
(\tau,t)
with
\tau\in(0,t)
, we obtain by using (
10.10
), Lemmas
10.2
–
10.4
and the Young inequality that

(10.25)

\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+aa_{1}\int^{t}_{\tau}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+Cc_{4}^{6}\int^{t}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+Cc_{4}^{9}t.

Thanks to
\eqref{ln}_{2}
, we have

\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}}+\|(h+\epsilon)L\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}\|_{L^{2}}\big)(\tau),

which, along with (
10.7
)–(
10.8
) and Lemma
10.1
, yields

\displaystyle\limsup_{\tau\to 0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|(\boldsymbol{u}_{0},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{g}_{*}\|_{L^{2}}+\|L\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq Cc^{2}_{0}.

Letting
\tau\to 0
in (
10.25
) and using the Grönwall inequality give

\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int^{t}_{0}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\big(c_{4}^{9}t+c^{4}_{0}\big)e^{Cc_{4}^{6}t}\leq Cc^{4}_{0}\qquad\text{for $t\in[0,T_{2}]$},

which, along with (
10.23
), yields

(10.26)

\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc_{2}^{4}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\|\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{D^{2}}\leq Cc_{2}^{5}c_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\int^{T_{2}}_{0}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq Cc^{5}_{0}.

Next, we see from (
10.21
) and Lemma
10.5
that

(10.27)

\|\mathcal{H}_{1}\|_{D^{1}}\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{1}}+c_{3}^{10}\big).

Then it follows from (
10.22
), (
10.27
), and Lemmas
10.3
,
10.5
, and
A.12
that

(10.28)

\displaystyle\|h\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{D^{3}}+\|\nabla^{3}\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\nabla^{2}\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{3}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{6}}+\|\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\mathcal{H}_{1}\|_{D^{1}}+c_{3}^{10}\big)\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{1}}+c_{3}^{10}\big),

which, along with (
10.26
), (
10.28
), and Lemma
10.4
, implies

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T_{2}}\|h\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq Cc^{5}_{0},\qquad\int_{0}^{T_{2}}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq Cc^{6}_{0}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Next, we establish the
D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-estimates for
\boldsymbol{u}
.

Lemma 10.7
.

Let
T_{3}:=\min\{T_{2},(1+Cc_{4})^{-28}\}
. Then, for any
t\in[0,T_{3}]
,

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int^{t}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc^{7}_{0},\qquad\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Cc^{8}_{0},

\displaystyle\|h\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{D^{1}}+\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq Cc^{11}_{3},

\displaystyle\int^{t}_{0}\big(\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{4}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{D^{2}}^{2}+\|(h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Cc^{11}_{0}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
Multiplying (
10.24
) by
\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
lead to

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\,\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(aa_{1}\|(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+a(a_{1}+a_{2})\|(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{div}\,\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big((\mathcal{H}_{0})_{t}-a\frac{h}{\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}}h_{t}L\boldsymbol{u}\Big)\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}a\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}_{t})\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\\
&\quad+\frac{a}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{h}{\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}}h_{t}\big(a_{1}|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\mathrm{div}\,\boldsymbol{u}_{t}|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\leq C\big(\|(\mathcal{H}_{0})_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\|h^{-1}h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}},\\
&\quad+C\big(\|\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+\|h^{-1}h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\\
&\leq Cc^{3}_{4}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+Cc^{17}_{4}.\end{aligned}

Integrating the above over
(\tau,t)
yields that, for
t\in[0,T_{3}]
,

(10.29)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int^{t}_{\tau}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\|(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+Cc_{4}^{3}\int^{t}_{0}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s+Cc_{4}^{17}t.

Due to
\eqref{ln}_{2}
, we have

\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}\leq\big(\|\sqrt{h}\nabla(\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{w}+\nabla\phi+a\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}L\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{w}))\|_{L^{2}}\big)(\tau).

Then it follows from (
10.7
), Lemma
10.1
, and Remark
10.1
that

\displaystyle\lim\sup_{\tau\to 0}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\sqrt{h_{0}}\nabla(\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{0})\|_{L^{2}}+\|\sqrt{h_{0}}\nabla^{2}\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\sqrt{h_{0}}\nabla(\boldsymbol{\psi}_{0}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}_{0}))\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathcal{G}_{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\sqrt{\varphi_{0}}\nabla^{3}u_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq Cc^{3}_{0},

where we have used the fact that

\sqrt{h_{0}}\nabla(\sqrt{h_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}L\boldsymbol{u}_{0})=\Big(\frac{h_{0}}{\sqrt{h_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}}\mathcal{G}_{2}+\epsilon^{2}\nabla L\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\frac{\sqrt{h_{0}}}{\sqrt{h_{0}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}}\Big).

Letting
\tau\to 0
in (
10.29
), we see from the Grönwall inequality that, for
t\in[0,T_{3}]
,

(10.30)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int^{t}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc^{7}_{0},\qquad\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq Cc^{8}_{0},

which, along with (
10.28
) and Lemmas
10.3
–
10.4
, yields

(10.31)

\|h\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{D^{1}}+\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc^{11}_{3}.

2.
We give the estimates for
(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u})
. First, it follows from Lemmas
10.5
–
10.6
and (
10.31
) that

(10.32)

\|\mathcal{H}_{2}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{4}^{9}\big).

Moreover, recall from the definition of
\mathcal{H}_{2}
and (
10.24
) that

(10.33)

aL(\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\boldsymbol{u}_{t})=\mathcal{H}_{2}.

Thus, based on (
10.19
), (
10.27
), (
10.30
),
and (
10.32
)–(
10.33
),
we obtain from the classical theory for elliptic equations in Lemma
A.12
, and Lemmas
10.3
–
10.6
that

(10.34)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{2}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla^{2}\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{3}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{6}}\big)

\displaystyle\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\mathcal{H}_{2}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{0}^{5}+c_{0}^{7}\big)\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{4}^{9}\big),

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|(h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})_{t}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+\|h^{-1}h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{4}^{9}\big),

\displaystyle\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{D^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{H}\|_{D^{2}}\leq C\big(c^{2}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{4}^{11}\big),

where, in the last inequality of (
10.34
), we have used the fact that

(10.35)

\mathcal{H}=a\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}L\boldsymbol{u}.

Next, notice from (
10.35
) that, for multi-index
\boldsymbol{\xi}=(\xi_{1},\cdots\!,\xi_{n})
with
|\boldsymbol{\xi}|=2
,

\displaystyle aL(\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\nabla^{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\boldsymbol{u})=\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\nabla^{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\big((\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}})^{-1}\mathcal{H}\big)-aG(\nabla\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}},\nabla^{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\boldsymbol{u}).

Then this, together with (
10.19
), (
10.27
), (
10.30
)–(
10.35
), the classical theory for elliptic equations in Lemma
A.12
, and Lemmas
10.3
–
10.6
, implies

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{D^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\nabla^{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\big((\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}})^{-1}\mathcal{H}\big)\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{3}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{6}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{2}}+c_{4}^{14}\big)\leq C\big(c_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+c_{4}^{14}\big).

Finally, it follows from the above, (
10.10
), (
10.30
), (
10.34
), and Lemma
10.4
that

\int^{T_{3}}_{0}\big(\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{4}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{D^{2}}^{2}+\|(h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq Cc^{11}_{0}.

This completes the proof.
∎

We now derive the time-weighted estimates for velocity
\boldsymbol{u}
.

Lemma 10.8
.

Let
T_{4}:=\min\{T_{3},(1+Cc_{5})^{-30}\}
. Then, for any
t\in[0,T_{4}]
,

\displaystyle t\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{4}}\big)(t)

\displaystyle\leq Cc^{24}_{4}

\displaystyle\int^{t}_{0}s\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{3}}+\|\sqrt{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq Cc^{22}_{4}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
Applying
\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\partial_{t}
to (
10.24
) and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
give

(10.36)

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+aa_{1}\|(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+a(a_{1}+a_{2})\|(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathrm{div}\,\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\sum^{2}_{i=1}J_{i}.

Here,
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
are given as follows and can be estimated by using Lemmas
10.5
and
A.1
, and the Hölder and Young inequities:

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\mathcal{H}_{0})_{tt}\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq\|(\mathcal{H}_{0})_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(c_{5}^{4}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+c^{-2}_{5}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{w}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\phi_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+c^{6}_{4}\big),

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(a\frac{h}{\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}}\nabla h\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}_{tt})+a\frac{h}{\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}}h_{tt}L\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\qquad+a\frac{\epsilon^{2}h^{2}_{t}}{(h^{2}+\epsilon^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}L\boldsymbol{u}+2a\frac{h}{\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}}h_{t}L\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\Big)\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}\|\varphi\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}+\|h^{-1}h_{tt}\|_{L^{2n}}\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{n-1}}}\big)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(\|h^{-1}h_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{2}}+\|h^{-1}h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}.

Multiplying
(
10.36
)
by
t
and integrating over
(\tau,t)
for
0<\tau<t\leq T_{4}
, together with the above estimates on
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
, (
10.10
) and Lemmas
10.2
–
10.7
, yield

(10.37)

t\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{aa_{1}}{4}\int^{t}_{\tau}s\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\tau\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}(\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+Cc^{20}_{4}+Cc^{4}_{5}\int^{t}_{\tau}s\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s.

To handle the right-hand side of (
10.37
), we see from (
10.30
) and Lemma
A.6
that there exists a sequence
s_{k}
such that
s_{k}\to 0
and
s_{k}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}(s_{k},\boldsymbol{x})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\to 0
as
k\to\infty
. Taking
\tau=s_{k}
and letting
k\to\infty
in
(
10.37
)
, we obtain from the Grönwall inequality that

(10.38)

t\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\int^{t}_{0}s\big(\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\sqrt{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}+\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc^{20}_{4}\quad\text{for $t\in[0,T_{4}]$}.

2.
It follows from (
10.34
) and (
10.38
) that

(10.39)

t^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+t^{\frac{1}{2}}\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+t^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq Cc^{12}_{4}.

Next, it follows from Lemmas
10.5
–
10.7
that

(10.40)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{H}_{2}\|_{D^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{L^{2}}+c^{4}_{4}\|\boldsymbol{w}_{t}\|_{D^{2}}+c^{4}_{4}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{2}}+c^{15}_{4}\big).

Hence, (
10.19
), (
10.33
), (
10.40
), and Lemmas
A.12
and
10.3
–
10.7
yield that, for
t\in[0,T_{4}]
,

\displaystyle\|h\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{3}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\nabla^{2}\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{3}}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{6}}+\|\nabla^{3}\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\mathcal{H}_{2}\|_{D^{1}}+c^{4}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{2}}+c_{4}^{8}\big),

which, along with (
10.38
)–(
10.40
), and Lemmas
10.4
and
10.7
, implies

\int^{T_{4}}_{0}s\big(\|\sqrt{h^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{D^{3}}^{2}+\|h\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc^{22}_{4}.

This completes the proof of Lemma
10.8
.
∎

It then follows from Lemmas
10.2
–
10.8
that, for
0\leq t\leq T_{4}=\min\{T^{*},(1+Cc_{5})^{-30}\}
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \big(\|\phi\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}\cap D^{1}\cap D^{3}}+\|\phi_{t}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\|\phi_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}\big)(t)+\int^{t}_{0}\big(\|\phi_{tt}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s&\leq Cc_{4}^{6},\\
\big(\|\nabla\sqrt{h}\|^{2}_{L^{2n}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}\cap D^{1,n}\cap D^{2}}\big)(t)\leq Cc^{2}_{0},\ \ \big(\|h^{-1}h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)(t)&\leq Cc_{3}^{\frac{9}{2}}c^{\frac{3}{2}}_{4},\\
\frac{2}{3}\eta^{-2\iota}<h^{-1}(t,\boldsymbol{x})<2c_{0},\ \big(\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\big)(t)+\int^{t}_{0}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s&\leq Cc^{4}_{0},\\
\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{D^{2}}^{2}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)(t)+\int^{t}_{0}\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{3}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s&\leq Cc_{2}^{10}c_{3},\\
\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}+\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{3}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}\big)(t)+\int^{t}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s&\leq Cc^{22}_{3},\\
\int^{t}_{0}\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{4}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+\|(h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s&\leq Cc^{22}_{3},\\
t\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{4}}\big)(t)&\leq Cc^{24}_{4},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int^{t}_{0}\big(s\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}+s\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{3}}+s\|\sqrt{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s&\leq Cc^{24}_{4}.\end{aligned}

Therefore, defining the time (
C\geq 4
: the generic constant)

T^{*}=\min\{T,(1+C^{1501}c^{4920}_{0})^{-30}\},

and constants

c_{1}^{2}=Cc^{2}_{0},\quad c_{2}^{2}=Cc^{4}_{0},\quad c_{3}^{2}=C^{6}c^{20}_{0},\quad c_{4}^{2}=C^{250}c^{820}_{0},\quad c_{5}^{2}=C^{3001}c^{9840}_{0},

we arrive at the following desirable estimates for
t\in[0,T^{*}]
:

\displaystyle\big(\|\phi\|^{2}_{D^{1}\cap D^{3}}+\|\phi_{t}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\|\phi_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}\big)(t)+\int^{t}_{0}\big(\|\phi_{tt}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{5}^{2},

\displaystyle\big(\|\nabla\sqrt{h}\|^{2}_{L^{2n}}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}\|^{2}_{D^{1,n}\cap D^{2}}\big)(t)\leq c^{2}_{1},\quad\big(\|h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)(t)\leq c_{4}^{2},

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}\eta^{-2\iota}\!<h^{-1}(t,\boldsymbol{x})<\!c_{1},\ \big(\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\big)(t)+\int^{t}_{0}\big(\|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{2}^{2},

(10.41)

\displaystyle\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{D^{2}}^{2}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)(t)+\int^{t}_{0}\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{3}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{3}^{2},

\displaystyle\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}+\|\sqrt{h}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{3}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}\big)(t)+\int^{t}_{0}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{4}^{2},

\displaystyle\int^{t}_{0}\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{4}}+\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+\|(h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{4}^{2},\quad t\|h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq c_{5}^{2},

\displaystyle t\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{D^{4}}\big)(t)+\int^{t}_{0}s\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}+\|\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{3}}+\|\sqrt{h}\boldsymbol{u}_{tt}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{5}^{2}.

10.3.
Inviscid Limit of the Artificial Viscosity

Based on the uniform estimates in (
10.41
), now we can obtain the local well-posedness of problem (
10.1
) with
\epsilon=0
.

Lemma 10.9
.

Let (
3.8
) hold,
\eta>0
, and
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})
satisfy (
3.9
)–(
3.10
). Then there exists
T^{*}>0
independent of
\eta
and a unique classical solution
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
in
[0,T^{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
of problem (
10.1
) with
\epsilon=0
satisfying (
10.3
) with
T
replaced by
T^{*}
, and
\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h^{\eta}
. Moreover, the uniform estimates in (
10.41
) that are independent of
\eta
still hold for
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
, and
C^{-1}\leq(gh^{-1})(t,\boldsymbol{x})\leq C
in
[0,T^{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1. Existence
. First, since
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})
satisfy (
3.9
)–(
3.10
), it follows from (
10.5
)–(
10.6
) that there exists a constant
c_{0}\geq 1
independent of
\eta
such that (
10.7
) holds. Then, according to Lemmas
10.1
–
10.8
, for any fixed
\epsilon>0
and
\eta>0
, there exist
T^{*}>0
independent of
(\epsilon,\eta)
and a unique classical solution
(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
in
[0,T^{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
of problem (
10.1
) satisfying the estimates in (
10.41
) which, along with
\eqref{ln}_{3}
, yields that, for
t\in[0,T^{*}]
,

(10.42)

\|h^{\epsilon,\eta}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla h^{\epsilon,\eta}(t)\|_{L^{2}}+\|h^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t}(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(A,\eta,a_{1},a_{2},\gamma,\delta,T^{*},\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0}).

Thus, it follows from (
10.41
)–(
10.42
) and the Aubin–Lions Lemma
A.9
that, for any
R>0
, there exists a subsequence of solutions (still denoted by)
(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta})
converging to a limit
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
as
\epsilon\to 0
:

(10.43)

(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta})\to(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})\quad\text{ in $C([0,T^{*}];H^{2}(B_{R}))$ \ as $\epsilon\to 0$},

where
B_{R}
is a ball centered at the origin with radius
R
, such that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}\qquad&\text{weakly* \ in }L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\\
(\nabla\phi^{\epsilon,\eta},\phi^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\epsilon,\eta},h^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t})\rightharpoonup(\nabla\phi^{\eta},\phi^{\eta}_{t},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta},h^{\eta}_{t})\qquad&\text{weakly* \ in }L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\\
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{t}\qquad&\text{weakly* \ in }L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\end{aligned}\hskip 2.27621pt

(10.44)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} t^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}^{\epsilon,\eta},\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta})\rightharpoonup t^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}^{\eta},\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta})\qquad&\text{weakly* \ in }L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\\
(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta}_{tt},t^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{tt})\rightharpoonup(\phi^{\eta}_{tt},t^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{tt})\qquad&\text{weakly* \ in }L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\\
(h^{\epsilon,\eta},(h^{\epsilon,\eta})^{-1}h^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t})\rightharpoonup(h^{\eta},(h^{\eta})^{-1}h^{\eta}_{t})\qquad&\text{weakly* \ in }L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\\
\nabla\sqrt{h^{\epsilon,\eta}}\rightharpoonup\nabla\sqrt{h^{\eta}}\qquad&\text{weakly* \ in }L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];L^{2n}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\\
\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{t}\qquad&\text{weakly \hskip 5.69054pt in }L^{2}([0,T^{*}];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \phi^{\epsilon,\eta}_{tt}\rightharpoonup\phi^{\eta}_{tt}\qquad&\text{weakly \hskip 5.69054pt in }L^{2}([0,T^{*}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\\
(\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{tt},\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{tt})\rightharpoonup(\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}_{tt},\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{tt})\qquad&\text{weakly \hskip 5.69054pt in }L^{2}([0,T^{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\\
t^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{tt},\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t})\rightharpoonup t^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{tt},\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{t})\qquad&\text{weakly \hskip 5.69054pt in }L^{2}([0,T^{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\end{aligned}\hskip 7.11317pt

which implies that
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
satisfies (
10.41
)–(
10.42
) except the weighted estimates on
\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}
.

Now, the uniform estimates on
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
obtained above and the convergences in (
10.43
)–(
10.3
) imply

(10.45)

\displaystyle\sqrt{h^{\epsilon,\eta}}(\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}_{t})\rightharpoonup\sqrt{h^{\eta}}(\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{t})

\displaystyle\text{weakly* in }L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle h^{\epsilon,\eta}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta}\rightharpoonup h^{\eta}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}

\displaystyle\text{weakly* in }L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle(h^{\epsilon,\eta}\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta},(h^{\epsilon,\eta}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\epsilon,\eta})_{t})\rightharpoonup(h^{\eta}\nabla^{4}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},(h^{\eta}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta})_{t})

\displaystyle\text{weakly \hskip 5.69054pt in }L^{2}([0,T^{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

which implies that
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
satisfies also the uniform weighted estimates of
\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}
in (
10.41
).

Now we are ready to show that
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
is a weak solution in the sense of distributions to (
10.1
) with
\epsilon=0
.
First, multiplying
\eqref{ln}_{1}
by any given
X(t,\boldsymbol{x})\in C^{\infty}_{c}([0,T^{*})\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
on both sides and integrating over
[0,T^{*})\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we have

\int_{0}^{T^{*}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\phi^{\epsilon,\eta}X_{t}-\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\phi^{\epsilon,\eta}X-(\gamma-1)\phi^{\epsilon,\eta}\diver\boldsymbol{w}X\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\mathrm{d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi_{0}^{\eta}X(0,\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=0.

According to the uniform estimates obtained above and the convergences shown in (
10.43
)–(
10.3
), we can pass to the limit
\epsilon\to 0
in the above to obtain

\int_{0}^{T^{*}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\phi^{\eta}X_{t}-\boldsymbol{w}\cdot\nabla\phi^{\eta}X-(\gamma-1)\phi^{\eta}\diver\boldsymbol{w}X\Big)\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\mathrm{d}t+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi_{0}^{\eta}X(0,\boldsymbol{x})\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=0.

Similarly, we can show that
(\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
also satisfies equations
\eqref{ln}_{2}
–
\eqref{ln}_{3}
and the initial data in the sense of distributions. Therefore,
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
is a weak solution of problem (
10.1
) with
\epsilon=0
in the sense of distributions, satisfying

\displaystyle\nabla\phi^{\eta}\in L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad 0<h^{\eta}\in L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}),\quad\nabla(\phi^{\eta})^{\iota}\in L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];L^{2n}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle(\nabla h^{\eta},h^{\eta}_{t})\in L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\ \ \ \boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}\in L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T^{*}];H^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T^{*}];D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\ \ \boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T^{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}\in L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];D^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad t^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T^{*}];D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle t^{\frac{1}{2}}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{tt}\in L^{\infty}([0,T^{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T^{*}];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})).

This implies that
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
satisfies equations
\eqref{ln}_{1}
–
\eqref{ln}_{3}
with
\epsilon=0
pointwisely.

2. Uniqueness and time continuity.
Due to
h^{\eta}>\frac{1}{2c_{0}}
, the uniqueness and the time continuity of the classical solution obtained above can be given by the same arguments used in Lemma
10.1
. Here we omit the details.
∎

10.4.
Nonlinear Approximation Solutions away from Vacuum

Now we give the local well-posedness of the classical solution to the following nonlinear problem when
\eta>0
:

(10.46)

\begin{cases}\phi^{\eta}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}\cdot\nabla\phi^{\eta}+(\gamma-1)\phi^{\eta}\mathrm{div}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}=0,\\[2.0pt]
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}+\nabla\phi^{\eta}+ah^{\eta}L\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}=\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}),\\[3.0pt]
h^{\eta}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}\cdot\nabla h^{\eta}+(\delta-1)h^{\eta}\mathrm{div}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}=0,\\[3.0pt]
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})|_{t=0}=(\phi^{\eta}_{0},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0},h_{0}^{\eta})=(\phi_{0}+\eta,\boldsymbol{u}_{0},(\phi_{0}+\eta)^{2\iota})\quad\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\[3.0pt]
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})\to(\eta,\boldsymbol{0},\eta^{2\iota})\quad\text{as $|\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty$}\qquad\text{for $t\geq 0$},\end{cases}

where
\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h^{\eta}
.

Theorem 10.1
.

Let (
3.8
) hold,
\eta>0
, and
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})
satisfy (
3.9
)–(
3.10
). Then there exist
T_{*}>0
independent of
\eta
and a unique classical solution

(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
of problem (
10.46
) satisfying (
10.3
), where
T_{*}
is independent of
\eta
. Moreover, the
\eta
-uniform estimates in (
10.41
) still hold for
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
with
T^{*}
replaced by
T_{*}
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
Since
(\phi_{0},\boldsymbol{u}_{0})
satisfy (
3.9
)–(
3.10
), it follows from (
10.5
)–(
10.6
) that there exists a constant
c_{0}\geq 1
independent of
\eta
such that (
10.7
) holds. Next, denote
\mathrm{X}=(\Phi,\mathrm{H})^{\top}
, and let
(\phi^{0},\boldsymbol{u}^{0},h^{0})
be the solution of the following Cauchy problem in
(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
:

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathrm{X}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\cdot\nabla\mathrm{X}=0,\\
\mathbf{U}_{t}-\mathrm{H}\Delta\mathbf{U}=\boldsymbol{0},\\
(\Phi,\mathbf{U},\mathrm{H})|_{t=0}=(\phi^{\eta}_{0},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0},h^{\eta}_{0})\quad\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\
(\Phi,\mathbf{U},\mathrm{H})\to(\eta,\boldsymbol{0},\eta^{2\iota})\quad\quad\ \ \ \ \text{as $|\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty$}\quad\text{for $t\geq 0$}.\end{cases}

Choose
\bar{T}\in(0,T^{*}]
small enough such that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} 0<(h^{0})^{-1}\leq c_{1},\ \ \sup_{t\in[0,\bar{T}]}\|\nabla h^{0}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}\cap D^{1,n}\cap D^{2}}&\leq c^{2}_{1},\\
\sup_{t\in[0,\bar{T}]}\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}(t)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\int^{\bar{T}}_{0}\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t&\leq c_{2}^{2},\end{aligned}

(10.47)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sup_{t\in[0,\bar{T}]}\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}\|_{D^{2}}^{2}+\|h^{0}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)(t)+\int^{\bar{T}}_{0}\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}\|^{2}_{D^{3}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c_{3}^{2},\\
\sup_{t\in[0,\bar{T}]}\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}+\|h^{0}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}\|^{2}_{D^{3}}\big)(t)+\int^{\bar{T}}_{0}\big(\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}\|^{2}_{D^{4}}+\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{tt}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c_{4}^{2},\\[-2.0pt]
\sup_{t\in[0,\bar{T}]}\|(h_{0})^{-1}h^{0}_{t}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}+\int^{\bar{T}}_{0}\big(\|(h^{0}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{0})_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|h^{0}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{0}\|^{2}_{D^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq c_{4}^{2},\\[4.0pt]
\sup_{t\in[0,\bar{T}]}\big(\|\sqrt{h^{0}}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|h^{0}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{0}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}\big)(t)\leq c^{2}_{4},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sup_{t\in[0,\bar{T}]}\big(t\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{t}(t)\|^{2}_{D^{2}}+t\|h^{0}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{t}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+t\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{tt}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+t\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}(t)\|^{2}_{D^{4}}\big)\leq c_{5}^{2},\\
\int^{t}_{0}\big(s\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{tt}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}+s\|\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{t}\|^{2}_{D^{3}}+s\|\sqrt{h^{0}}\boldsymbol{u}^{0}_{tt}\|_{D^{1}}^{2}+\|h^{0}_{tt}\|^{2}_{D^{1}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c_{5}^{2}.\end{aligned}

2. Existence
. Let
(\boldsymbol{w},g)=(\boldsymbol{u}^{0},h^{0})
in problem (
10.1
) with
\epsilon=0
. Then, according to Lemma
10.9
,
we can obtain a unique classical solution
(\phi^{1},\boldsymbol{u}^{1},h^{1})
, which solves this problem locally in time. Inductively, we construct approximate sequences
(\phi^{k+1},\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1},h^{k+1})
as follows:
given
(\boldsymbol{u}^{k},h^{k})
for
k\geq 1
, define
(\phi^{k+1},\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1},h^{k+1})
by solving the linear problem:

(10.48)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\phi^{k+1}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\nabla\phi^{k+1}+(\gamma-1)\phi^{k+1}\mathrm{div}\boldsymbol{u}^{k}=0,\\[2.0pt]
\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{t}^{k+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k}+\nabla\phi^{k+1}+ah^{k+1}L\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1}=\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k+1}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}^{k}),\\[2.0pt]
\displaystyle h^{k+1}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\nabla h^{k+1}+(\delta-1)h^{k}\mathrm{div}\boldsymbol{u}^{k}=0,\\[2.0pt]
\displaystyle(\phi^{k+1},\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1},h^{k+1})|_{t=0}=(\phi^{\eta}_{0},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0},h^{\eta}_{0})\qquad\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\[2.0pt]
\displaystyle(\phi^{k+1},\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1},h^{k+1})\to(\eta,\boldsymbol{0},\eta^{2\iota})\quad\quad\ \qquad\text{as $|\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty$}\quad\text{for $t\geq 0$},\end{cases}

where
\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k+1}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h^{k+1}
.
It follows from Lemma
10.9
that the solution
(\phi^{k+1},\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1},h^{k+1})

(k\in\mathbb{N})
satisfies the uniform estimates (
10.41
) and
C^{-1}\leq(h^{k}(h^{k+1})^{-1})(t,\boldsymbol{x})\leq C
in
[0,\bar{T}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
To show the strong convergence of
(\phi^{k},\boldsymbol{u}^{k},h^{k})
, we set

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}^{k+1}=\phi^{k+1}-\phi^{k},\ \ \bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}=\boldsymbol{u}^{k+1}-\boldsymbol{u}^{k},\ \ \bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}=\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k+1}-\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k},\ \ \bar{h}^{k+1}=h^{k+1}-h^{k}.

Then
(\phi^{k},\boldsymbol{u}^{k},h^{k})
solves the following problem due to (
10.48
):

(10.49)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\bar{\phi}^{k+1}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\nabla\bar{\phi}^{k+1}+\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\cdot\nabla\phi^{k}+(\gamma-1)\big(\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\mathrm{div}\boldsymbol{u}^{k}+\phi^{k}\mathrm{div}\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\big)=0,\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}_{t}^{k+1}+\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}+\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1}+\nabla\bar{\phi}^{k+1}+ah^{k+1}L\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}+a\bar{h}^{k+1}L\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\\[2.0pt]
\displaystyle\quad=\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}^{k})+\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\cdot Q(\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}),\\[2.0pt]
\displaystyle\bar{h}^{k+1}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\nabla\bar{h}^{k+1}+\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\cdot\nabla h^{k}+(\delta-1)\big(h^{k}\mathrm{div}{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}-{h}^{k-1}\mathrm{div}\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1}\big)=0,\\[2.0pt]
\displaystyle(\bar{\phi}^{k+1},\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1},\bar{h}^{k+1})|_{t=0}=(0,\boldsymbol{0},0)\qquad\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\[4.0pt]
\displaystyle(\bar{\phi}^{k+1},\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1},\bar{h}^{k+1})\to(0,\boldsymbol{0},0)\ \ \ \ \qquad\text{as $|\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty$}\quad\text{for $t\geq 0$}.\end{cases}

Besides, by the same argument as in the proof of
[
19
, Lemma 3.11]
, we can obtain the following regularity for
(\bar{\phi}^{k+1},\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1},\bar{h}^{k+1})
:

(\bar{\phi}^{k+1},\ \bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1},\ \bar{h}^{k+1})\in L^{\infty}([0,\bar{T}];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\qquad\text{for $k\in\mathbb{N}$}.

Moreover, it follows from the relation
\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k+1}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h^{k+1}
and
\eqref{k+1}_{3}
that

(10.50)

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}_{t}+\nabla\big(\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}+\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k}\big)+(\delta-1)\big(\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k}\mathrm{div}\boldsymbol{u}^{k}+\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k-1}\mathrm{div}\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\big)

\displaystyle+a\delta\big(h^{k}\nabla\diver\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}+\bar{h}^{k}\nabla\mathrm{div}\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1}\big)=\boldsymbol{0}.

2.1. Estimates for
\varphi^{k+1}\bar{h}^{k+1}
.
Denote
\varphi^{k}=(h^{k})^{-1}
. Then, according to
\eqref{k+1}_{3}
,

\varphi^{k+1}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\cdot\nabla\varphi^{k+1}+(1-\delta)h^{k}(\varphi^{k+1})^{2}\diver\boldsymbol{u}^{k}=0.

Multiplying
\eqref{k+3}_{3}
by
2\overline{h}^{k+1}(\varphi^{k+1})^{2}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
yield

(10.51)

\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\|\varphi^{k+1}\bar{h}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\\
\leq\frac{C}{\sigma}\|\varphi^{k+1}\bar{h}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\sigma\big(\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\,\sqrt{h^{k}}\diver\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\,\varphi^{k}\bar{h}^{k})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big).

2.2. Estimates for
(\bar{\phi}^{k+1},\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1},\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1})
.
First, it follows from
\eqref{k+3}_{2}
that

(10.52)

\displaystyle\|h^{k}\nabla^{2}\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\,\sqrt{h^{k}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\,\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}_{t})\|_{L^{2}}+\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k-1}\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\|\sqrt{h^{k-1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k-1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k},\,\varphi^{k}\bar{h}^{k},\,\nabla\bar{\phi}^{k})\|_{L^{2}}\big),

where we have used the fact that
\nabla\varphi^{k}=-(\varphi^{k})^{2}\nabla h^{k}
and the following estimates:

(10.53)

\displaystyle\|\varphi^{k}\bar{h}^{k}\|_{L^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\varphi^{k}\bar{h}^{k}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla(\varphi^{k}\bar{h}^{k})\|_{L^{2}}\big)\leq C\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k},\,\varphi^{k}\bar{h}^{k})\|_{L^{2}}\big),

\displaystyle\|\bar{h}^{k}L\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\|\varphi^{k}\bar{h}^{k}h^{k}L\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1}\|_{L^{2}}=\|(\varphi^{k}\bar{h}^{k})(h^{k-1}L\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1})((h^{k-1})^{-1}h^{k})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\varphi^{k}\bar{h}^{k}\|_{L^{3}}\|h^{k-1}L\boldsymbol{u}^{k-1}\|_{L^{6}}\|(h^{k-1})^{-1}h^{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Next, multiplying (
10.50
) by
2\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, combined with (
10.53
), give that, for any
\sigma\in(0,1)
,

(10.54)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\sqrt{h^{k}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},h^{k}\nabla^{2}\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k},\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1})\|_{L^{2}}+\|\varphi^{k}\bar{h}^{k}\|_{L^{3}}\big)\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sigma}\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\sigma\big(\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\,\sqrt{h^{k}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\,\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}_{t})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k-1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle+\sigma\big(\|\sqrt{h^{k-1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k-1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k},\,\varphi^{k}\bar{h}^{k},\,\nabla\bar{\phi}^{k})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big).

For the estimates of
\bar{\phi}^{k+1}
, multiplying
\eqref{k+3}_{1}
by
2\bar{\phi}^{k+1}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
give

(10.55)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\,\sqrt{h^{k}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k})\|_{L^{2}}\|\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}.

Furthermore, applying
2\partial^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}_{\boldsymbol{x}}\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\partial^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}_{\boldsymbol{x}}

(|\boldsymbol{\varsigma}|=1)
to
\eqref{k+3}_{1}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we obtain

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial^{\boldsymbol{\varsigma}}_{\boldsymbol{x}}\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+C\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\,\sqrt{h^{k}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\,h^{k}\nabla^{2}\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k})\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}},

which, along with (
10.52
) and (
10.55
), implies that, for
t\in[0,\bar{T}]
,

(10.56)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\leq\frac{C}{\sigma}\|\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\sigma\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\,\sqrt{h^{k}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\,h^{k}\nabla^{2}\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

We now estimate
\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}
. Multiplying
\eqref{k+3}_{2}
by
2\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
yield

(10.57)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\!+\!aa_{1}\|\sqrt{h^{k+1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sigma}\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\big(\|\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{H^{1}}+\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle+\sigma\big(\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\,\sqrt{h^{k}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\!+\!\|\varphi^{k+1}\bar{h}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big),

where we have used (
10.53
) and the fact that

\|h^{k+1}L\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{6}}\leq C\|(h^{k})^{-1}h^{k+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h^{k}L\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\|_{L^{6}}.

Multiplying
\eqref{k+3}_{2}
by
2\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}_{t}^{k+1}
and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
yield

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\big(aa_{1}\|\sqrt{h^{k+1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+a(a_{1}+a_{2})\|\sqrt{h^{k+1}}\mathrm{div}\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)+2\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\sqrt{h^{k}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\sqrt{h^{k+1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1},\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1},\varphi^{k+1}\bar{h}^{k+1})\big\|_{L^{2}}\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+C\|\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{H^{1}}\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\sqrt{h^{k+1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

which, along with the Young inequality, leads to

(10.58)

\displaystyle\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\big(aa_{1}\|\sqrt{h^{k+1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+a(a_{1}+a_{2})\|\sqrt{h^{k+1}}\mathrm{div}\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)+\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\big\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\sqrt{h^{k}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k},\sqrt{h^{k+1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1},\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1},\varphi^{k+1}\bar{h}^{k+1})\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big).

2.3. Closing the estimates.
Combining (
10.54
) with (
10.56
)–(
10.58
) implies

(10.59)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\varGamma^{k+1}(t,\upsilon_{1})+aa_{1}\|\sqrt{h^{k+1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\upsilon_{1}\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}_{t}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sigma}\varGamma^{k+1}(t,\upsilon_{1})\!+\!C\sigma\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k}_{t}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\!+\!C(\sigma+\upsilon_{1})\big(\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k-1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\!+\!\|\sqrt{h^{k-1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k-1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\!+\!\varGamma^{k}(t,\upsilon_{1})\big),

where
\upsilon_{1}\in(0,1)
is a constant to be determined later and

\displaystyle\varGamma^{k+1}(t,\upsilon_{1})

\displaystyle=\|\bar{\phi}^{k+1}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\varphi^{k+1}\bar{h}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\upsilon_{1}aa_{1}\|\sqrt{h^{k+1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\upsilon_{1}a(a_{1}+a_{2})\|\sqrt{h^{k+1}}\mathrm{div}\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Set
\sigma=\upsilon_{1}^{\frac{3}{2}}
and define

\mathcal{E}^{k+1}(t,\upsilon_{1}):=\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\varGamma^{k+1}(s,\upsilon_{1})+\int^{t}_{0}\big(aa_{1}\|\sqrt{h^{k+1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\upsilon_{1}\|\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k+1}_{t}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s.

Then it follows from (
10.59
) and the Grönwall inequality that

(10.60)

\mathcal{E}^{k+1}(t,\upsilon_{1})\leq C\upsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}_{1}e^{C\upsilon_{1}^{-\frac{3}{2}}t}(t+1)\big(\mathcal{E}^{k}(t,\upsilon_{1})+\mathcal{E}^{k-1}(t,\upsilon_{1})\big).

Since
\bar{T}\in(0,1)
, we can first choose
\upsilon_{1}=\bar{\upsilon}\in(0,1)
such that

C\bar{\upsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{1}{64}
, and then choose
T_{*}\in(0,\bar{T}]
such that

(1+T_{*})e^{C\bar{\upsilon}^{-\frac{3}{2}}T_{*}}\leq 4
. Then (
10.60
) becomes

\mathcal{E}^{k+1}(T_{*},\bar{\upsilon})\leq\frac{1}{16}\big(\mathcal{E}^{k}(T_{*},\bar{\upsilon})+\mathcal{E}^{k-1}(T_{*},\bar{\upsilon})\big),

which yields
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{E}^{k}(T_{*},\bar{\upsilon})<\infty
.

This, combined with the
k
-uniform estimates (
10.41
), gives that, for any
s^{\prime}\in[1,3)
,

\|(\bar{\phi}^{k},\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}^{k})\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}+\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}^{k},\bar{h}^{k})\|_{L^{\infty}}\to 0\qquad\text{as $k\to\infty$},

which implies that there exist a subsequence (still denoted by
(\phi^{k},\boldsymbol{u}^{k},h^{k},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k})
) and limit functions
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta})
such that

\displaystyle\phi^{k}\to\phi^{\eta}

\displaystyle\text{in $L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))$},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}^{k}\to\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}

\displaystyle\text{in $L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];H^{s^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))$},

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k},h^{k})\to(\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta},h^{\eta})

\displaystyle\text{in $L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))$}.

On the other hand, the local estimates (
10.41
) independent of
k
yield that there exists a subsequence (still denoted by)
(\phi^{k},\boldsymbol{u}^{k},h^{k},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{k})
converging to the limit
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta})
in the weak or weak* sense.
According to the lower semi-continuity of norms, the corresponding estimates in (
10.41
) still hold for
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta})
with
T^{*}
replaced by
T_{*}
. Thus, we can check that
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
is a weak solution of (
10.46
) in the sense of distributions and satisfies the equations in (
10.46
) pointwise.

3. Uniqueness and time-continuity.
Let
(\phi_{1},\boldsymbol{u}_{1},h_{1})
and
(\phi_{2},\boldsymbol{u}_{2},h_{2})
be two classical solutions to the Cauchy problem (
10.46
) satisfying the estimates in (
10.41
).

Set

\boldsymbol{\psi}_{i}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h_{i}
(
i=1,2
), and

\displaystyle\bar{h}=h_{1}-h_{2},\qquad\bar{\phi}=\phi_{1}-\phi_{2},\qquad\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}=\boldsymbol{u}_{1}-\boldsymbol{u}_{2},\qquad\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}=\boldsymbol{\psi}_{1}-\boldsymbol{\psi}_{2}.

Then it follows from the equations in (
10.46
) that

(10.61)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\cdot\nabla\bar{\phi}+\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\nabla\phi_{2}+(\gamma-1)(\bar{\phi}\mathrm{div}\boldsymbol{u}_{1}+\phi_{2}\mathrm{div}\bar{\boldsymbol{u}})=0,\\[3.0pt]
\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\cdot\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}+\nabla\bar{\phi}+ah_{1}L\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}\\[3.0pt]
\displaystyle\quad=-\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{2}-a\bar{h}L\boldsymbol{u}_{2}+\boldsymbol{\psi}_{1}\cdot Q(\bar{\boldsymbol{u}})+\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}_{2}),\\[3.0pt]
\bar{h}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}_{1}\cdot\nabla\bar{h}+\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}\cdot\nabla h_{2}+(\delta-1)(\bar{h}\mathrm{div}\boldsymbol{u}_{2}+h_{1}\mathrm{div}\bar{\boldsymbol{u}})=0,\\[3.0pt]
\displaystyle(\bar{\phi},\bar{\boldsymbol{u}},\bar{h})|_{t=0}=(0,\boldsymbol{0},0)\qquad\text{in $\mathbb{R}^{n}$},\\[3.0pt]
\displaystyle(\bar{\phi},\bar{\boldsymbol{u}},\bar{h})\to(0,\boldsymbol{0},0)\qquad\quad\ \text{as $|\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty$}\quad\text{for $t\geq 0$}.\end{cases}

Set

\varGamma(t)=\|\bar{\phi}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|(h_{1})^{-1}\bar{h}\|_{L^{2}}+\|(\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}},\,\sqrt{aa_{1}h_{1}}\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}},\,\sqrt{a(a_{1}+a_{2})h_{1}}\diver\bar{\boldsymbol{u}},\bar{\boldsymbol{u}})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}
.
In a similar way for the derivation of (
10.60
), we obtain

\varGamma^{\prime}(t)+C^{-1}\|(\nabla\bar{\boldsymbol{u}},\,\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}_{t})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\leq\bar{H}(t)\varGamma(t),

with a continuous function
\bar{H}(t)\in L^{1}(0,T_{*})
. Hence, it follows from the Grönwall inequality that

\bar{\phi}=0
and
\bar{\boldsymbol{\psi}}=\bar{\boldsymbol{u}}=\boldsymbol{0}
.

Finally, the time-continuity follows from the same procedure as in Lemma
10.1
. Thus, the proof of Theorem
10.1
is completed.
∎

10.5.
Passing to the Limit
\eta\to 0

Based on the uniform estimates in (
10.41
), we are ready to prove Theorem
3.1
.

Proof of
Theorem
3.1
.

We divide the proof into four steps.

1. Local uniform lower bound of
\phi
. For any
\eta\in(0,1]
, set

\phi_{0}^{\eta}=\phi_{0}+\eta,\qquad\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}_{0}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla(\phi_{0}+\eta)^{2\iota},\qquad h_{0}^{\eta}=(\phi_{0}+\eta)^{2\iota}.

First, since (
3.9
)–(
3.10
) hold, it follows from (
10.5
)–(
10.6
) that there exists a constant
c_{0}\geq 1
independent of
\eta
such that (
10.7
) holds.
Therefore, it follows from Theorem
10.1
that, for the initial data
(\phi^{\eta}_{0},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{0},h^{\eta}_{0})
, problem (
10.46
) admits a unique classical solution
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta})
in
[0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying the estimates in (
10.41
), where
T_{*}
is independent of
\eta
and
\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h^{\eta}
.

Moreover,
\phi^{\eta}
is locally uniformly positive, as shown below, which can be proved by the same argument for Lemma 3.12 in
[
19
]
.

Lemma 10.10
.

For any
R_{0}>0
and
\eta\in(0,1)
, there exists a constant
C(R_{0})
independent of
\eta
such that

\phi^{\eta}(t,x)\geq C(R_{0})^{-1}>0\qquad\text{for all $(t,\boldsymbol{x})\in[0,T_{*}]\times B_{R_{0}}$}.

2. Passing to the limit
\eta\to 0
. First, it follows from the
\eta
-independent estimates in (
10.41
) and Lemma
A.9
that,
for any
R>0
, there exists a subsequence (still denoted by (
\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},h^{\eta},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}
)) converging to a limit
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},h,\boldsymbol{\psi})
as follows:

(10.62)

\displaystyle\phi^{\eta}-\eta\to\phi

\displaystyle\text{in}\ \ C([0,T_{*}];D^{1}(B_{R})),\quad

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}\to\boldsymbol{\psi}\ \ \text{in}\ \ C([0,T_{*}];D^{1,n}(B_{R})),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}\to\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle\text{in}\ \ C([0,T_{*}];H^{2}(B_{R})),\quad

\displaystyle h^{\eta}\to h\ \ \text{in}\ \ C([0,T_{*}];H^{2}(B_{R})).

Moreover, since
\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h^{\eta}
, we see that
\boldsymbol{\psi}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h
.

Second, the
\eta
-independent estimate (
10.41
) also yields that
there exists a subsequence (still denoted by)
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},\nabla\sqrt{h^{\eta}},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta})
converging to
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\tilde{h}},\boldsymbol{\psi})
as follows:

(10.63)

\displaystyle\phi^{\eta}-\eta\rightharpoonup\phi

\displaystyle\text{weakly}^{*}\ \ \!\text{in }L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle\text{weakly}\ \,\,\hskip 2.84526pt\text{in }L^{2}([0,T_{*}];H^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{\psi}

\displaystyle\text{weakly}^{*}\ \ \!\text{in }L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{1,n}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle\nabla\sqrt{h^{\eta}}\rightharpoonup\boldsymbol{\tilde{h}}

\displaystyle\text{weakly}^{*}\ \ \!\text{in }L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{2n}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle\phi_{t}^{\eta}\rightharpoonup\phi_{t}

\displaystyle\text{weakly}^{*}\ \ \!\text{in }L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{u}_{t}^{\eta},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t}^{\eta})\rightharpoonup(\boldsymbol{u}_{t},\boldsymbol{\psi}_{t})

\displaystyle\text{weakly}^{*}\ \ \!\text{in }L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})).

Then, by the lower semi-continuity of weak convergences,
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
satisfies the corresponding estimates as in (
10.41
) except the estimate on
\nabla\sqrt{h}
and the
h
-weighted ones on
\boldsymbol{u}
.
In fact, it follows from the
\eta
-independent estimate (
10.41
) for
(\phi^{\eta},\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},\boldsymbol{\psi}^{\eta})
, the facts that
h\geq(2c_{0})^{-1}
and
h^{\eta}\geq(2c_{0})^{-1}
, and the convergences shown in (
10.62
)–(
10.63
) that
\boldsymbol{\tilde{h}}=\nabla\sqrt{h}
and

\displaystyle\sqrt{h^{\eta}}(\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}_{t})\rightharpoonup\sqrt{h}(\nabla\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u}_{t})

\displaystyle\text{weakly}^{*}\ \ \!\text{in}\ \ L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle h^{\eta}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}\rightharpoonup h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle\text{weakly}^{*}\ \ \!\text{in}\ \ L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle h^{\eta}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta}\rightharpoonup h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle\text{weakly}\ \ \hskip 4.2679pt\!\text{in}\ \ L^{2}([0,T_{*}];D^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

\displaystyle(h^{\eta}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}^{\eta})_{t}\rightharpoonup(h\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})_{t}

\displaystyle\text{weakly}\ \ \hskip 4.2679pt\!\text{in}\ \ L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})).

Hence, the corresponding weighted estimates for
u
in (
10.41
) still hold for the limit functions, and it is direct to show that
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},h)
satisfy the equations in
\eqref{eq:cccq}_{1}
and

h_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla h+(\delta-1)h\mathrm{div}\,\boldsymbol{u}=0\qquad\text{holds pointwise}.

Then, denoting
\phi^{*}=h-\phi^{2\iota}
, we have

\phi^{*}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\phi^{*}+(\delta-1)\phi^{*}\diver\boldsymbol{u}=0,\qquad\phi^{*}(0,\boldsymbol{x})=0\ \ \text{for $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$},

which, along with the characteristic method, implies that
\phi^{*}=0
.
Therefore,
h=\phi^{2\iota}
.

Hence,
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
is a weak solution of problem (
3.2
)–(
3.6
) in the sense of distributions and satisfies system (
3.2
) pointwisely.

3. Proof for
\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\in C([0,T];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
.

Let
f(s)\in C^{\infty}([0,\infty))
be a function such that

f(s)>0,\qquad f^{\prime}(s)\leq 0,\qquad f(s)=1\quad\text{for $s\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]$},\qquad f(s)=e^{-s}\quad\text{for $s\in[1,\infty)$}.

Clearly, there exists a generic constant
C>0
such that
|f^{\prime}(s)|\leq Cf(s)
. Moreover, for any
R>1
, define
f_{R}(\boldsymbol{x})=f(\tfrac{|\boldsymbol{x}|}{R})
.

Since equation
\eqref{eq:cccq}_{1}
holds pointwisely everywhere, we can multiply it by
f_{R}(\boldsymbol{x})\phi^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma-1}}
and integrate over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\diver(\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\boldsymbol{u})f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\boldsymbol{x}f^{\prime}(\frac{|\boldsymbol{x}|}{R})\frac{1}{R|\boldsymbol{x}|}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R}\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x},

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, yields

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}_{0}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}_{0}\|_{L^{1}}

with
C
a generic constant independent of
R
.
Note that
\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}f_{R}\to\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}
as
R\to\infty
for all
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Thus, by the Fatou lemma (Lemma
A.5
), we have

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\liminf_{R\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C\|\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}_{0}\|_{L^{1}},

which implies that
\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
.

To show the time-continuity, we see from
\eqref{eq:cccq}_{1}
that

(\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}})_{t}=-\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\diver\boldsymbol{u}-(a\delta)^{-1}\phi^{\frac{2-\delta}{\gamma-1}}\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}.

Then, by the regularity properties of
(\phi,\boldsymbol{\psi},\boldsymbol{u})
, we obtain that
(\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}})_{t}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
, so that
\phi^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\in C([0,T];L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
.

4. Uniqueness and time-continuity
. The uniqueness follows from the same procedure as in the proof of Theorem
10.1
, and the time-continuities of
(\phi,\boldsymbol{\psi})
can be obtained by a similar argument as in Lemma
10.1
. Moreover, since the vector
\boldsymbol{\psi}
is spherically symmetric, we see from Lemma
C.2
that

(10.64)

(\boldsymbol{\psi},\nabla\phi^{2\iota})\in C([0,T_{*}];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}})).

For velocity
\boldsymbol{u}
, the
a priori
estimates obtained above and Lemmas
A.1
and
A.8
imply

(10.65)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T_{*}];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\qquad\phi^{\iota}\nabla\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})).

It then follows from
\eqref{eq:cccq}_{2}
that

\phi^{-2\iota}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\quad(\phi^{-2\iota}\boldsymbol{u}_{t})_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),

which, along with Lemma
A.8
, implies that
\phi^{-2\iota}\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\in C([0,T_{*}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
. This and the classical elliptic estimates in Lemma
A.12
for

\displaystyle aL\boldsymbol{u}

\displaystyle=-\phi^{-2\iota}(\boldsymbol{u}_{t}+\boldsymbol{u}\cdot\nabla\boldsymbol{u}+\nabla\phi-\boldsymbol{\psi}\cdot Q(\boldsymbol{u}))

yield that
\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T_{*}];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
immediately.

Finally, note that

\phi^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))\cap L^{2}([0,T_{*}];D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})),\qquad(\phi^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u})_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})).

Thus, Lemma
A.8
gives
\phi^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T_{*}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}))
which, combined with the facts that
\nabla\phi^{2\iota}\in C([0,T_{*}];C(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}}))
and
\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T_{*}];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
, yields

(\phi^{2\iota}\nabla^{2}\boldsymbol{u},\phi^{2\iota}\nabla^{3}\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})).

Finally,
\boldsymbol{u}_{t}\in C([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
follows directly from the above, (
10.64
)–(
10.65
), the time continuities of
(\phi,\boldsymbol{\psi})
, and
\boldsymbol{u}\in C([0,T_{*}];H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
.
∎

10.6.
The Proof for Theorem
3.2

Based on Theorem
3.1
, now we are ready to establish the local well-posedness of the regular solution of the original Cauchy problem (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with (
1.6
)–(
1.7
) shown in Theorem
3.2
.

Proof for Theorem
3.2
.

It follows from the initial assumptions (
2.5
)–(
2.6
) and Theorem
3.1
that there exists
T_{*}>0
such that problem (
3.2
)–(
3.6
) has a unique regular solution
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
satisfying the regularity property (
2.7
) with
T
replaced by
T_{*}
, which implies

\phi\in C^{1}([0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}),\qquad(\boldsymbol{u},\nabla\boldsymbol{u})\in C([0,T_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{n}).

Set
\rho=(\frac{\gamma-1}{A\gamma}\phi)^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}>0
with
\rho(0,\boldsymbol{x})=\rho_{0}
. Due to
\boldsymbol{\psi}=\frac{a\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla h
and
h=\phi^{2\iota}
, we see that
\boldsymbol{\psi}=\frac{\delta}{\delta-1}\nabla\rho^{\delta-1}
. Hence, based on the above regularity property and relations of solution
(\phi,\boldsymbol{u},\boldsymbol{\psi})
, multiplying
\eqref{eq:cccq}_{1}
by

\frac{\partial\rho}{\partial\phi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})=\frac{1}{\gamma-1}\Big(\frac{\gamma-1}{A\gamma}\Big)^{\frac{1}{\gamma-1}}\phi^{\frac{2-\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(t,\boldsymbol{x})

yields equation
\eqref{eq:1.1}_{1}
, while multiplying
\eqref{eq:cccq}_{2}
by
\rho
gives equation (
1.1
).

Therefore, we have shown that
(\rho,\boldsymbol{u})
satisfies problem (
1.1
)–(
1.4
) with (
1.6
)–(
1.7
) in the sense of distributions and has the regularity properties shown in Definition
2.1
and (
2.7
).
∎

Appendix A
Some Basic Lemmas

We present some useful lemmas in this appendix, which are used frequently in the previous sections. The first lemma is on the classical Sobolev embedding theorem.

Lemma A.1

(
[
1
,
13
]
)
.

Assume that
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}

(n\in\mathbb{N}^{*})
is a domain with smooth boundary. Let
f\in W^{k,p}(\Omega)
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
p\in[1,\infty]
.

\mathrm{(i)}

Let
kp\leq n
. Then
W^{k,p}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{s}(\Omega)
for any
s\in\big[p,\frac{np}{n-kp}\big]
if
kp<n
and any
s\in[p,\infty)
if
kp=n
, and there exists
C_{1}>0
depending only on
(k,p,s,n,\Omega)
such that

\|f\|_{L^{s}(\Omega)}\leq C_{1}\|f\|_{W^{k,p}(\Omega)}.

In particular, if
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
kp<n
, and
f\in D^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap L^{\frac{np}{n-kp}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, or
f\in D^{k,p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
f\to 0
as
|\boldsymbol{x}|\to\infty
, then

\|f\|_{L^{\frac{np}{n-kp}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C_{1}\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

\mathrm{(ii)}

Let
(k,p)=(n,1)
. Then
W^{n,1}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow C(\overline{\Omega})
and there exists
C_{2}>0
depending only on
(n,\Omega)
such that

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C_{2}\|f\|_{W^{n,1}(\Omega)}.

\mathrm{(iii)}

Let
kp>n
. Then
W^{k,p}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow C^{\ell}(\overline{\Omega})
for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
0\leq\ell<k-n/p
, and there exists
C_{3}>0
depending only on
(k,p,\ell,n,\Omega)
such that

\max_{0\leq j\leq\ell}\|\nabla^{j}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C_{3}\|f\|_{W^{k,p}(\Omega)},

where
C^{\ell}(\overline{\Omega})

(\ell\in\mathbb{N})
denotes the space of all functions
f
for which
\nabla^{j}f

(0\leq j\leq\ell)
are bounded and uniformly continuous in
\Omega
. In particular, the following inequality holds for
f=f(r)\in H^{1}(0,R)

(R>0)
:

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(0,R)}^{2}\leq(1+R^{-1})\|f\|_{L^{2}(0,R)}^{2}+\|f_{r}\|_{L^{2}(0,R)}^{2}.

Moreover, if
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{n}
, then the above constants
(C_{1},C_{2},C_{3})
are independent of
\Omega
.

The second lemma concerns the well-known Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality.

Lemma A.2

(
[
47
]
)
.

Assume that
f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{\ell,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for
1\leq p,q\leq\infty
. Let real numbers
(\vartheta,s)
and natural numbers
(n,\ell,j)
satisfy

\frac{1}{{s}}=\frac{j}{n}+\Big(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{\ell}{n}\Big)\vartheta+\frac{1-\vartheta}{p},\qquad\frac{j}{\ell}\leq\vartheta\leq 1.

Then
f\in D^{j,{s}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and there exists
C>0
depending only on
(\ell,n,j,p,q,\vartheta)
such that

\|\nabla^{j}f\|_{L^{{s}}}\leq C\|\nabla^{\ell}f\|_{L^{q}}^{\vartheta}\|f\|_{L^{p}}^{1-\vartheta}.

Moreover, if
j=0
,
\ell q<n
, and
p=\infty
, then it is necessary to make the additional assumption that either f tends to zero at infinity or that f lies in
L^{\tilde{s}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for some finite
\tilde{s}>0
;
if
1<q<\infty
and
\ell-j-n/q
is a non-negative integer, then it is necessary to assume also that
\vartheta\neq 1
.

The third lemma is on the fundamental theorem of calculus. This lemma plays an important role in justifying the procedure of integration by parts in spherical coordinates.

Lemma A.3

(
[
49
]
)
.

The following statements hold
:

(i)

Let
l_{1}\in(0,\infty)
and
l_{2}\in[0,\infty)
. Assume that
f(r)\in W^{1,1}(0,l_{1})
and
g(r)\in L^{p}(l_{2},\infty)\cap D^{1,1}(l_{2},\infty)
for some
p\in[1,\infty)
. Then, for any
r_{0},r_{1}\in[0,l_{1}]
and
r_{2}\in[l_{2},\infty)
,

(A.1)

f(r_{1})=f(r_{0})+\int_{r_{0}}^{r_{1}}f_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r,\qquad g(r_{2})=-\int_{r_{2}}^{\infty}g_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r.

In particular,
g(r)\to 0
as
r\to\infty
and the following estimates hold
:

(A.2)

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(0,l_{1})}\leq\|f_{r}\|_{L^{1}(0,l_{1})}\quad\text{if $f(0)=0$},\qquad\,\,\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(l_{2},\infty)}\leq\|g_{r}\|_{L^{1}(l_{2},\infty)}.

(ii)

If
f
is a function such that
r^{m}(f_{r},\frac{f}{r})\in L^{1}(I)
, then

(A.3)

\int_{0}^{\infty}(r^{m}f)_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=0.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1. Proof for (i).
(
A.2
) is a direct consequence of (
A.1
), and
\eqref{newton}_{1}
is the classical fundamental theorem of calculus; see
[
49
, Chapter 7]
. Hence, it suffices to prove
\eqref{newton}_{2}
.

We first see from Lemma
A.1
that
g\in C([l_{2},l_{3}])
for any
l_{3}\in[l_{2},\infty)
. Thus,
g(r)
is well-defined on
[l_{2},\infty)
. Next, for any
g\in L^{p}(l_{2},\infty)\cap D^{1,1}(l_{2},\infty)
, by the fundamental theorem of calculus, we obtain that, for any fixed
r_{2},r_{3}\in[l_{2},\infty)
,

(A.4)

g(r_{3})=g(r_{2})+\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{3}}g_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r.

Taking the limit as
r_{3}\to\infty
gives

L:=\lim_{r_{3}\to\infty}|g(r_{3})|\leq|g(r_{2})|+\int_{r_{2}}^{\infty}|g_{r}|\,\mathrm{d}r\leq|g(r_{2})|+\|g_{r}\|_{L^{1}(l_{2},\infty)}<\infty.

We claim that the limit
L=0
. Otherwise, if
L>0
, we can find a constant
M>0
such that, for any
r>M
,
|g(r)|\geq\frac{L}{2}
. This yields

\int_{l_{2}}^{\infty}|g|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}r\geq\int_{M}^{\infty}|g|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}r\geq\int_{M}^{\infty}\big(\frac{L}{2}\big)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}r=\infty,

which contradicts the fact that
g\in L^{p}(l_{2},\infty)
.

Therefore,
L=0
, and we obtain from (
A.4
) that

g(r_{2})=\lim_{r_{3}\to\infty}\Big(g(r_{3})-\int_{r_{2}}^{r_{3}}g_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)=-\int_{r_{2}}^{\infty}g_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r.

2. Proof for (ii).
Define
\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x})=f(r)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{r}
. Then
\boldsymbol{f}
is a spherically symmetric vector function defined on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and Lemmas
B.1
and
C.1
(see Appendices
B
–
C
) yield
\boldsymbol{f}\in L^{\frac{n}{n-1}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Moreover, via the coordinate transformation, we have

\int_{0}^{\infty}(r^{m}f)_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r=\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{m}\big(f_{r}+\frac{m}{r}f\big)\,\mathrm{d}r=\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\diver\boldsymbol{f}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x},

where
\omega_{n}
denotes the surface area of the
n
-D sphere, satisfying
\omega_{2}=2\pi
and
\omega_{3}=4\pi
.

Next, define
B_{R}:=\{\boldsymbol{x}:|\boldsymbol{x}|<R\}
. Let
\varphi(\boldsymbol{x})\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(B_{2})
be a cut-off function such that
\varphi\equiv 1
in
B_{1}
and
|\nabla\varphi|\leq C
for some constant
C>0
, and let
\varphi_{R}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\varphi(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{R})
. Then we obtain from the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\diver\boldsymbol{f}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big|

\displaystyle=\lim_{R\to\infty}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\diver\boldsymbol{f})\varphi_{R}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big|=\lim_{R\to\infty}\Big|\frac{1}{R}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\boldsymbol{f}\cdot(\nabla\varphi)\big(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{R}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{C}{R}\int_{B_{2R}\backslash B_{R}}|\boldsymbol{f}|\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C\lim_{R\to\infty}\Big(\int_{B_{2R}\backslash B_{R}}|\boldsymbol{f}|^{\frac{n}{n-1}}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big)^{\frac{n-1}{n}}=0.

This completes the proof.
∎

The fourth lemma is on the Hardy inequality.

Lemma A.4

(
[
6
,
13
,
48
]
)
.

Let
q\in[2,\infty)
,
b>0
, and let
f=f(r)
be a function defined on
[0,b]
such that
r^{\ell+1+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}}(f,f_{r})\in L^{q}(0,b)
for some
p\in[q,\infty]
and
\ell>-\frac{1}{p}

(\ell>0
if
p=\infty)
. Then
r^{\ell}f\in L^{p}(0,b)
and the following inequalities hold
:

\displaystyle\|r^{\ell}f\|_{L^{p}(0,b)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\big\|r^{\ell+1+\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}}(f,f_{r})\big\|_{L^{q}(0,b)}

\displaystyle\text{if }p\in[q,\infty),

\displaystyle\|r^{\ell}f\|_{L^{\infty}(0,b)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\big\|r^{\ell+1-\frac{1}{q}}(f,f_{r})\big\|_{L^{q}(0,b)}

\displaystyle\text{if }p=\infty,

where
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are positive constants depending only on
(\ell,p,q,b)
and
(\ell,q,b)
, respectively. Moreover, if
r^{\ell+1-\frac{1}{q}}(f,f_{r})\in L^{q}(0,b)
for some
\ell>0
, then
r^{\ell}f\in C([0,b])
.

The fifth lemma is the well-known Fatou lemma which can be found in
[
49
]
.

Lemma A.5

(
[
49
]
)
.

Let
\{f_{n}\}
be a sequence of measurable non-negative functions
f_{n}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Define
f(\boldsymbol{x}):=\liminf_{n\to\infty}f_{n}(\boldsymbol{x})
for a.e.
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then
f
is measurable and

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f_{n}\,\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{x}\qquad\text{for {\it a.e.} $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$}.

The sixth lemma is used to obtain the time-weighted estimates of the velocity.

Lemma A.6

(
[
2
,
13
]
)
.

Let
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}

(n\in\mathbb{N}^{*})
be any set and
f\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}(E))
. Then there exists a sequence
\{t_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
such that

t_{k}\to 0,\quad\ t_{k}\|f(t_{k})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(E)}\to 0\qquad\,\,\text{as $k\to\infty$}.

The seventh lemma gives an equivalent statement on the spherically symmetric vector fields.

Lemma A.7
.

Let
\boldsymbol{f}=\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x})
be a spherically symmetric continuous vector function on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then
\boldsymbol{f}
takes the form
:

\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x})=f(|\boldsymbol{x}|)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{|\boldsymbol{x}|}
if and only if

(A.5)

\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x})=\boldsymbol{f}(\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x})\qquad\text{for all $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$ and $\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)$}.

In particular, any spherically symmetric vector function
\boldsymbol{f}
satisfies
\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{0})=\boldsymbol{0}
.

Proof.

Clearly, if
\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x})=f(|\boldsymbol{x}|)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{|\boldsymbol{x}|}
, then (
A.5
) holds.

Conversely, if (
A.5
) holds, we take the 3-D case as an example. Let
\boldsymbol{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be any fixed displacement vector,
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{0}}{|\boldsymbol{x}_{0}|}
, and let
\mathcal{O}_{1}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
be a rotation by 180 degrees about an axis parallel to
\boldsymbol{x}_{0}
,
i.e.
,
\mathcal{O}_{1}\boldsymbol{x}_{0}=\boldsymbol{x}_{0}
. Then (
A.5
) implies

(A.6)

\mathcal{O}_{1}\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x}_{0})=\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x}_{0}).

Next, suppose that
\{\boldsymbol{e}_{2},\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\}
are unit vectors that, together with
\boldsymbol{e}_{1}
, form an orthonormal basis for
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Then there exist constants
\alpha_{i}=\alpha_{i}(\boldsymbol{x}_{0})\in\mathbb{R}

(i=1,2,3)
, depending only on
\boldsymbol{x}_{0}
, such that
\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x}_{0})=\alpha_{1}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}+\alpha_{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{2}+\alpha_{3}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}
. This, combined with (
A.6
), gives

\alpha_{1}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}+\alpha_{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{2}+\alpha_{3}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}=\alpha_{1}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}-\alpha_{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{2}-\alpha_{3}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}\implies\alpha_{2}\boldsymbol{e}_{2}+\alpha_{3}\boldsymbol{e}_{3}=\boldsymbol{0}\implies\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}=0.

Hence, for any fixed
\boldsymbol{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x}_{0})=\alpha_{1}\boldsymbol{e}_{1}=\alpha_{1}(\boldsymbol{x}_{0})\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{0}}{|\boldsymbol{x}_{0}|}.

By (
A.5
), we see that
\alpha_{1}(\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{x}_{0})=\alpha_{1}(\boldsymbol{x}_{0})
for all
\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)
, that is,
\alpha_{1}(\boldsymbol{x}_{0})=\alpha_{1}(|\boldsymbol{x}_{0}|)
.
Thus, letting
f(r):=\alpha_{1}(r)
implies that
\boldsymbol{f}
takes the form:
\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{x})=f(|\boldsymbol{x}|)\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{|\boldsymbol{x}|}
.

Finally, from (
A.5
), we can take
\boldsymbol{x}=\boldsymbol{0}
to obtain

(A.7)

\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{0})=\mathcal{O}\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{0})\qquad\text{for all $\mathcal{O}\in\mathrm{SO}(n)$}.

Then, choosing
\mathcal{O}=\mathcal{O}_{2}
by 180 degrees with respect to
an axis perpendicular to
\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{0})
, that is,
\mathcal{O}_{2}\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{0})=-\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{0})
, we obtain from (
A.7
) that
\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{0})=-\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{0})
, so that
\boldsymbol{f}(\boldsymbol{0})=\boldsymbol{0}
.
∎

The following lemma is on the evolution triple embedding.

Lemma A.8

(
[
21
]
)
.

Let
T>0
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
n\geq 2
.
Suppose that
f\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
and
f_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
.
Then
f\in C([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
, and the mapping
:

t\mapsto|f(t)|_{2}^{2}
is absolutely continuous
with

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{2}=2\left<f_{t},f\right>_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\times H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\qquad\text{for {\it a.e.} $t\in(0,T)$}.

Moreover, if additionally
f\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
, then
f\in C([0,T];L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
for
q\in[2,\infty)
if
n=2
and for
q\in[2,\frac{2n}{n-2})
if
n\geq 3
.

The following lemma is on the Aubin–Lions lemma.

Lemma A.9
.

[
53
]

Let
X_{0}\subset X\subset X_{1}
be three Banach spaces. Suppose that
X_{0}
is compactly embedded in
X
, and
X
is continuously embedded in
X_{1}
. Then the following statements hold
:

(i)

If
F
is bounded in
L^{p}([0,T];X_{0})
for
1\leq p<\infty
, and
F_{t}
is bounded in
L^{1}([0,T];X_{1})
, then
F
is relatively compact in
L^{p}([0,T];X)
;

(ii)

If
F
is bounded in
L^{\infty}([0,T];X_{0})
, and
F_{t}
is bounded in
L^{p}([0,T];X_{1})
for
p>1
, then
F
is relatively compact in
C([0,T];X)
.

The following auxiliary lemma is used to show some equivalent norms for a function
f

satisfying
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\nabla f^{\alpha}\in D^{1,n}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

Lemma A.10
.

Let
n=2,3
, and let
f>0
be a spherically symmetric scalar function defined on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. If
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\nabla f^{\alpha}\in D^{1,n}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap D^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for some
\alpha\in(-\frac{1}{n},0)
, then

\mathrm{(i)}

f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for all
p\in(1,\infty]
;

\mathrm{(ii)}

\nabla f^{\alpha}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\nabla f^{\beta}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for all
\beta\in[\alpha+\frac{1}{2},\infty)
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

1.
Since
f
is a spherically symmetric scalar function, then
\nabla f^{\alpha}
is also a spherically symmetric vector function.

First, we obtain from Lemma
C.2
in Appendix
C
that

(A.8)

\|\nabla f^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\nabla f^{\alpha}\|_{D^{1,n}}\leq C.

Next, let
n^{*}
be defined as in §
3.1.3
. Then it follows from (
A.8
), Lemma
A.2
, and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{4-n}{n+4}}\|\nabla^{2}f\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{2n}{n+4}}\leq C\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{4-n}{n+4}}\big(\|f^{1-2\alpha}|\nabla f^{\alpha}|^{2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|f^{1-\alpha}\nabla^{2}f^{\alpha}\|_{L^{2}}\big)^{\frac{2n}{n+4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{4-n}{n+4}}\big(\|f\|_{L^{2-4\alpha}}^{\frac{2n(1-2\alpha)}{n+4}}\|\nabla f^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{4n}{n+4}}+\|f\|_{L^{n^{*}(1-\alpha)}}^{\frac{2n(1-\alpha)}{n+4}}\|\nabla^{2}f^{\alpha}\|_{L^{n}}^{\frac{2n}{n+4}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{4}{n+4}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{n(1-4\alpha)}{n+4}}\|\nabla f^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{4n}{n+4}}+C\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{2}{n+4}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{n+2-2n\alpha}{n+4}}\|\nabla^{2}f^{\alpha}\|_{L^{n}}^{\frac{2n}{n+4}},

which, along with the Young inequality, implies

(A.9)

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{1+n\alpha}}\|\nabla f^{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{n}{1+n\alpha}}+C\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{1}{1+n\alpha}}\|\nabla^{2}f^{\alpha}\|_{L^{n}}^{\frac{n}{1+n\alpha}}\leq C.

Therefore,
f\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, which, along with
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, gives (i).

2.
It remains to show that
\nabla f^{\beta}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for all
\beta\in[\alpha+\frac{1}{2},\infty)
. A direct calculation gives

\displaystyle(f^{\beta})_{x_{i}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta}{\alpha}f^{\beta-\alpha}(f^{\alpha})_{x_{i}},\qquad(f^{\beta})_{x_{i}x_{j}}=\frac{\beta}{\alpha}\Big(\frac{\beta-\alpha}{\alpha}f^{\beta-2\alpha}(f^{\alpha})_{x_{i}}(f^{\alpha})_{x_{j}}+f^{\beta-\alpha}(f^{\alpha})_{x_{i}x_{j}}\Big),

\displaystyle(f^{\beta})_{x_{i}x_{j}x_{k}}
